# DECISION_LOG — Brambleloop Studio

Consequential decisions with reasoning, so future sessions do not re-litigate them.

| # | Date | Decision | Why | Reversible? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B-001 | 2026-09-17 | Project-Money 90-day competition retired; Brambleloop Studio is the active mission. | Owner directive: "Scrap the competition. Competition is cancelled." Handoff package v1.2 supplied as canonical spec. | Owner's call |
| B-002 | 2026-09-17 | Python 3.11 for the core, not Node/TS. | The differentiating work is deterministic arithmetic, PDF/chart generation and image compositing. Python's reportlab/Pillow are already proven working in this environment. A JS frontend is not needed for an admin dashboard. | Yes, but costly |
| B-003 | 2026-09-17 | Postgres-backed durable queue (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), not Redis/Celery/SQS. | Master Plan section 13 demands durable jobs with leases, retries and idempotency. Postgres already has to exist for the warehouse; adding a second datastore adds a component that can die unattended at 3am with nobody watching. Fewer moving parts is a reliability decision, not a laziness one. | Yes |
| B-004 | 2026-09-17 | SQLite for tests, Postgres for production, behind one abstraction. | Acceptance tests must run hermetically anywhere, including before any cloud account exists. SKIP LOCKED degrades to a transactional claim on SQLite. | Yes |
| B-005 | 2026-09-17 | Writer and reverse compiler share **no** parsing code. | If they shared a grammar, a round-trip test would prove only that the bug is symmetric. Master Plan section 3 requires the reverse compiler to see "only the customer pattern". Independence is the whole point. | No — this is load-bearing |
| B-006 | 2026-09-17 | Digital twin refuses to model a pattern that failed compilation. | A twin built on broken arithmetic is fiction, and every downstream gate (Asset Truth, size claims, yardage) would then be reasoning about an object that cannot exist. | No |
| B-007 | 2026-09-17 | Yardage is an estimate with an explicit ±20% tolerance and a `calibrated` flag. | Yarn consumption genuinely varies by yarn, hook and tension. Inventing a precise number would be the exact "unsupported claim" the Policy Gate exists to block. Physical tests calibrate it (Master Plan section 3). | Yes, as data arrives |
| B-008 | 2026-09-17 | Magic ring modelled as a `foundation_kind`, not a free-text note. | The writer was emitting "sc in next 6 sts" for a magic-ring round — nonsense to a maker. An override string would have created a hole in reverse-compilation verification; a structural field keeps the check intact. | Yes |
| B-009 | 2026-09-17 | Built under `brambleloop/` in the Project-Money repo rather than a new repo. | Push credentials are scoped to `StateFarm91/Project-Money`. Blocking real progress on repo cosmetics would violate the directive's "do not stop because one integration is unavailable; route around it". Migration to a dedicated repo is trivial later and is tracked in BUILD_STATE. | Yes |
| B-010 | 2026-09-17 | Cloud not provisioned yet despite a verified Railway account. | Master Plan section 35 defers paid infrastructure approval until the system reaches the integration that needs it. Provisioning an empty project now would spend the owner's money to host nothing. Deploy when there is a service worth running. | Yes |
| B-011 | 2026-09-17 | Opportunity scoring is a weighted sum of six named components, computed in code. No model ranks concepts. | A model is good at proposing concepts and unreliable at being consistent about why one beats another. "The model preferred it" is not a reason the owner can overrule six weeks later when a SKU underperforms. Every score decomposes into demand, competition headroom, verifiability, seasonal fit, margin and whitespace, and the weights are one editable dict. | Yes — weights are data |
| B-012 | 2026-09-17 | Seasonal fit is evaluated at **our ready date**, using **the concept's own** maker hours, not the event date and not generic ones. | Two separate errors this avoids. Ranking by days-until-the-holiday would schedule Christmas blankets for December, when no buyer can finish one. Using the event's generic make time would give a stocking and a throw the same deadline, and they are six weeks apart. Scoring at today's date rather than the date we could actually list is a third: a concept we cannot ship for three weeks must be judged on the market that will exist in three weeks. | Yes |
| B-013 | 2026-09-17 | Portfolio selection applies structural constraints and will deliberately take a lower-scoring concept to meet them. | Section 33 forbids a first portfolio that depends on a single demand pattern. Pure ranking produced four Christmas items and no fast movers. Every swap is recorded with both scores and the constraint it served, so the trade is visible rather than buried. | Yes |
| B-014 | 2026-09-17 | Bundles are derived inventory: excluded from the ranked competition, appended only when their family earned at least two places. | The bundle scores highest in the pool because it is cheap and high-margin — but only once its members exist. Ranking it as an independent SKU produced a portfolio containing a bundle of products we had not built, which is a listing we cannot fulfil. | Yes |
| B-015 | 2026-09-17 | Competitor profiles carry an observation date and a URL, and are asserted on in tests. | Market evidence rots silently. An undated price band read as current a year later is worse than no data, because it looks like data. | No |
| B-016 | 2026-09-17 | Stitch taxonomy carries `height` (turning-chain units) and `row_height` (physical) as separate fields. | They were one field. A dc turns with three chains and is about twice the height of a sc; conflating them overstated a blanket's finished length by half. Finished length is a size claim on a listing, so this was a refund waiting to be printed, not a rounding issue. | No |
| B-017 | 2026-09-17 | Yarn constants are anchored on a checkable reference, and the anchor is written down next to them. | The first table was set by feel and came out at less than half of a defensible figure — a throw would have been advertised as needing 190 m. An estimate is allowed to be wrong by its stated tolerance; being wrong by a factor of two is not an estimate. Still uncalibrated at +/-20% until physical tests replace it. | Yes, on evidence |
| B-018 | 2026-09-17 | The flagship's pattern is generated from a motif grid, and each row is emitted as a repeat rather than flattened. | Flattening twelve repeats of a twelve-stitch motif produced a row instruction eleven lines long: physically unusable, and the kind of thing that makes a maker lose their place and blame the pattern. The repeat form is both what a human follows and what the compiler checks for divisibility, so readable and verifiable are the same form. | Yes |
| B-019 | 2026-09-17 | Large charts are published as one detected repeat plus a placement note, never as the whole piece. | A 144 x 120 chart on one page gives each stitch about a pixel. The repeat is *computed* from the fabric rather than asserted in copy, so the chart cannot claim a repeat the pattern does not have. | Yes |
| B-020 | 2026-09-17 | Web, worker and scheduler run in one container for now. | Railway bills by resources consumed and the company has no revenue; three always-on services cost roughly three times one. Correctness does not depend on the split — the queue is durable with leases and idempotency keys — so splitting later is a start command, and `worker_entry.py` and `scheduler_entry.py` already exist for that day. | Yes |
| B-021 | 2026-09-17 | With `BRAMBLELOOP_REQUIRE_POSTGRES=1` the app refuses to start rather than fall back to SQLite. | A container that silently falls back to a file on ephemeral disk passes every health check, renders the dashboard, and destroys every job, audit record and release certificate on its next restart. Failing to start is enormously preferable to a company whose memory quietly resets. | No |
| B-022 | 2026-09-17 | Artifact bytes are stored on local disk; artifact hashes are stored in Postgres. | No object storage is provisioned. The split means the system can always prove the PDF it is about to ship is the one that was certified, and when a file is gone it knows it is gone instead of serving a different one. Every run audits `assets.storage_not_durable` rather than implying durability it does not have. | Yes, swap in S3 |
| B-023 | 2026-09-17 | Prompts are immutable, content-hashed and referenced as `name@version`. | A prompt is a dependency, not a string. If it can be edited in place then two runs of the same job are not comparable, a regression cannot be attributed, and the audit trail records a call to something that no longer exists. Editing means publishing a new version; the old text stays so last month's output can still be explained. | No |
| B-024 | 2026-09-17 | No registered prompt may ask a model for pattern content, and this is enforced by a test. | Section 2 is absolute and a standing constraint is worth nothing if it lives only in a docstring. The prompt set is deliberately narrow: naming, readability polish that may not change a fact, and review mining. Everything load-bearing stays deterministic. | No |
| B-025 | 2026-09-17 | `ModelGateway.require_deterministic()` raises rather than returning a model verdict. | Section 27 says deterministic validation wins even if every LLM disagrees. Making it a method rather than a comment means a future caller reaching for a model opinion on pattern correctness gets an exception instead of a code review that may not happen. | No |
| B-026 | 2026-09-17 | The gateway ships with a deterministic `EchoProvider` and reports zero real providers. | No API key exists in this environment. Echo mocks the transport — pinning, parsing, cost, failover, circuit breaking — never judgement, which eval fixtures test instead. `available_providers()` returning an empty list is the truthful answer, and the dashboard shows it. | Yes, when a key exists |
| B-027 | 2026-09-17 | Agent permissions and ceilings are declarative: `seed_defaults` reconciles them on every boot. | Insert-if-absent meant a permission fixed in code never reached a database that already had the agent. Production proved it: the heartbeat fix deployed and the heartbeat kept failing. A permission that lives in code and cannot reach the database is not a permission, it is a comment. Runtime state such as `enabled` is left alone. | No |
| B-028 | 2026-09-17 | The container becomes healthy before it takes on load (`BRAMBLELOOP_RUNNER_START_DELAY`). | Two deployments were marked failed by the platform while working perfectly: the embedded worker began a full planning cycle at startup, and rendering eleven products' PDFs is CPU-bound work holding the GIL, which starved the health endpoint past the timeout. Serving first and working second is how a container is supposed to start. | Yes |
| B-029 | 2026-09-17 | Dead letters can be re-driven, but a closed capability gate can never be. | A dead letter from a fixed defect is work the company still owes, and leaving it red for a day teaches an operator to ignore the signal. Re-driving a shadow-mode publish refusal, by contrast, is an operator repeatedly asking a closed gate to open. A permission denial IS re-drivable: it reads like a refusal and is usually a misconfiguration, and the worker re-authorises on dispatch so a still-missing permission simply fails again. | Yes |
| B-030 | 2026-09-17 | `/api/verify` evaluates the standing safety assertions against live state and returns 503 if any fails. | "Railway says deployed" is not "the company is alive and behaving". The questions an absent owner actually has — is it running, is its memory durable, has anything escaped shadow mode, are the money guards on — are now answerable in one request, with the evidence for each check attached so a green result can be argued with. | No |
| B-031 | 2026-09-17 | The brand is data — palette, type, crop rules, naming grammar, character bible — not taste. | Consistency maintained by judgement alone does not survive a system producing assets unattended for months. `check_grid_coherence` can say whether the storefront still reads as one shop without anyone looking at it. | Yes |
| B-032 | 2026-09-17 | The brand model is refused for product-first categories, refused any real-person likeness, and can never be the hero. | Section 6 says product-first imagery is stronger for blankets and decor, and a model holding a blanket hides the pattern being sold. A likeness of a real person is refused however the request is phrased. An AI lifestyle concept as hero would be a picture of a thing nobody has made presented as evidence the thing exists. | No |
| B-033 | 2026-09-17 | Listing imagery is part of the release: rendered from the twin, class-declared, Asset Truth-checked, and blocking. | Imagery assembled after certification is imagery nothing verified. Rendering every frame from the same twin as the PDF means no frame can assert something the pattern does not produce, and a listing whose imagery misrepresents the pattern now stops the chain before pricing. | No |
| B-034 | 2026-09-17 | Search coverage is scored against *reachable* queries, not the biggest ones. | A shop with no history is not placing for "crochet blanket pattern", and scoring against it would tell the system to spend all thirteen tag slots on queries it will never appear for. Technique tags are gated on techniques the pattern actually uses: ranking for something we are not is a bounce, not a win. | Yes |
| B-035 | 2026-09-17 | Pricing optimises expected contribution per visitor, never gross revenue or lowest price. | A cheaper pattern converts better and can still earn less per visitor; a dearer one can earn more in total while killing the review accumulation a new shop depends on. Every figure is net of Etsy's real fee stack, because a decision made on gross is made on a number that never reaches the bank. | Yes |
| B-036 | 2026-09-17 | A price experiment's stopping rule is fixed before it starts, and a sub-8% price difference is refused at design time. | A test whose stopping rule is decided while looking at the results is a way of confirming what the operator already believed. At the sample sizes a new shop can reach, a 3% price difference is unmeasurable. | No |
| B-037 | 2026-09-17 | Thumbnail composition is checked now; hero ranking waits for real impressions and refuses without them. | Section 7 asks for both. The compositional half works today because a hero can be downscaled to Etsy's real grid size and measured. The evidence half cannot: inventing plausible CTRs to make a ranking function return something is the fabricated engagement section 8 forbids, applied to ourselves. | Yes, when data exists |
| B-038 | 2026-09-17 | Support triage puts escalation first in the routing table, and every reply is recorded as unsent. | A message containing both "chargeback" and "yarn" is a dispute; ordering is what stops a legal matter reaching the materials desk. And "we could not have sent it" is a weaker guarantee than "we recorded that we did not". | No |
| B-039 | 2026-09-17 | The portfolio classifier returns NO_EVIDENCE rather than a diagnosis for a SKU nobody has seen. | Every SKU has zero impressions. A classifier that read that as CONVERSION_PROBLEM would retire a catalogue that has simply never been shown to anyone — the most expensive mistake available to this component. | No |
| B-040 | 2026-09-17 | Confidence is tracked as seven separate dimensions with deliberately no overall score. | An average is more confident than the guess and less confident than the proof. Arithmetic confidence is near-total and yardage confidence is 0.45; averaging them would let a listing imply the yarn figure is as solid as the stitch counts. Physical confidence starts at zero and no amount of computation moves it. | No |
| B-041 | 2026-09-17 | Regression fixtures store the defective pattern, not the fix, and a clean pattern cannot become one. | A suite made of fixed patterns proves only that the fixed patterns are still fixed. A fixture that provokes no finding asserts nothing, and a suite full of those reports green while testing nothing. | No |
| B-042 | 2026-09-17 | Products are generated from a validated motif library; a width the motif cannot tile is refused. | Engineering twelve products by hand is twelve chances to slip. A truncated motif compiles perfectly and leaves half a fir tree down one side — a defect no arithmetic will ever catch, so the builder refuses rather than fudges. | Yes |
| B-043 | 2026-09-17 | A bundle has no pattern of its own and is assembled from its members' certified releases. | The pipeline had been certifying a "Nordic Forest Collection Bundle" whose PDF was a twelve-row striped panel: a product sold as three patterns and delivered as one invented swatch. A collection now lists only members that actually shipped, and waits when fewer than two have. | No |
| B-044 | 2026-09-17 | The books are built while every figure is zero, and the tax reserve is never counted as ours. | Accounting that first appears alongside the first sale is accounting nobody has checked; accounting built while the answer is obviously zero can be checked against an answer everyone knows. GST/HST is money held for a government, and counting it as revenue is how a small business discovers a liability it has already spent. | No |
| B-045 | 2026-09-17 | The CA$100K trajectory refuses to produce a run rate or a projection from zero orders. | It is the number most likely to be quoted back as though it were a forecast. With no orders there is no run rate; what the function returns instead is arithmetic on the target, labelled as arithmetic. | No |
| B-046 | 2026-09-17 | The CFO/Skeptic reports concerns even when nothing is wrong. | A reviewer who only speaks up during a crisis is a reviewer nobody has calibrated. It blocks on infrastructure above the owner's ceiling and on advertising with no attributable orders, and notes the burn and the tax reserve every time. | Yes |
| B-047 | 2026-09-17 | Paid-media guard rails are built now, while no campaign exists and no money can be spent. | Advertising is the one part of this system that can lose money quickly and silently. Guard rails written after the first campaign are guard rails written by someone who already knows what they cost. `authorise_spend` refuses on phase and on missing owner authority before it looks at any budget: a cap is not permission. | No |
| B-048 | 2026-09-17 | Broken tracking pauses a campaign before any budget question is asked. | A campaign running with broken attribution is not one that might be losing money; it is one nobody can tell is losing money, and a budget cap does not protect against being blind. | No |
| B-049 | 2026-09-17 | Every acceptance-gate line has its own named test. | "Gate B passes" was previously inferred from a scattering of component tests. Now each line of the owner's acceptance document has a test that names it, so a gate claim is checkable, and a second Gate F test asserts that nothing actually graduated. | No |
| B-050 | 2026-09-17 | Difficulty claims are checked in both directions. | The rule only caught "beginner" on a Class C pattern, which left the gate line "unsupported difficulty claims are blocked" half true. Overstating difficulty is just as unsupported as understating it, and it turns away the buyer the product was designed for. | Yes |
| B-051 | 2026-09-17 | Every idempotency key after certification carries a chain version. | Production proved the need: fifteen products were certified before listing imagery, search coverage and the content ecosystem existed, and the next cycle collapsed every downstream job against keys that were already taken — reporting success and producing nothing. Certification is correctly once per version; everything after it is different work when the code that does it changes. | Yes |
| B-052 | 2026-09-17 | `chain.rebuild` runs hourly and restarts certified releases that have no current listing. | Bumping the chain version makes the keys free; something still has to notice. A certified product with no listing is a product that cannot be sold, which is worth checking for on a cadence rather than only after a deploy. | Yes |
| B-053 | 2026-09-17 | A listing-copy change bumps the chain version so it reaches products already shipped. | The first title generator produced "Pet Snuggle Mat \| ... \| Pet Pet \| ..." and it went to production. A stuttering title is the clearest possible signal that nobody read the listing before it went up, and fixing the generator without bumping the chain would have left the stutter on every shipped product. | Yes |
| B-054 | 2026-09-17 | `create_all` is followed by an additive schema migration that adds missing columns and their indexes. | `create_all` does not touch a table that already exists, so a column added in code never reaches a deployed database. This is the third instance of one problem — code changed, the deployed database did not — after the agent permission and the release chain. Additive only: it will not drop, narrow, rename or backfill, and it refuses a NOT NULL column with no default rather than guessing. | No |
| B-055 | 2026-09-17 | Listings record the chain version that produced them, and the rebuild restarts stale ones, not only missing ones. | The first rebuild asked whether a listing *existed*. A stale listing exists, so a copy fix reached nothing and every shipped product kept a stuttering title through two deploys. Staleness has to be visible to be actionable. | No |
| B-056 | 2026-09-17 | An added column is backfilled with the model's literal default. | Without it a fresh database has the column NOT NULL with a value and a migrated one has it NULL, so the same code reads two different shapes — and the comparison deciding whether a row is stale silently compares against None. Only literal defaults are backfilled; a callable default is per-row logic this module has no business guessing. | No |
| B-057 | 2026-09-17 | Row-level repeats are derived from the compiled rows, never declared in the CIR. | A declared repeat is a second statement of the same fact, and two statements can disagree while both stay internally consistent — no arithmetic would catch it, because both halves add up. `detect_cycle` computes the cycle from the rows the compiler already validated, so there is no field to get wrong and nothing to keep in sync. The cost is that an intended repeat the designer wants stated but the rows do not support cannot be expressed; that is the right refusal. | No |
| B-058 | 2026-09-17 | The reverse compiler parses and expands the repeat instruction itself, from the customer text alone. | The obvious shortcut is for the writer to hand the reader its expansion. Then the two are no longer independent and the whole validation chain checks its own work (B-005). So the reader has its own regex and its own expansion, and it refuses a repeat that points at rows the text never printed. A reader that ignored the instruction instead would report every collapsed pattern as ninety rows short, which is worse than not supporting repeats at all. | No |
| B-059 | 2026-09-17 | A piece worked in the round is measured as a surface of revolution, not as flat fabric. | The flat model reads stitches-around as a width. Thirty stitches at 20 sts/10cm is 15 cm of *circumference*, which is a piece under 5 cm across, so a listing built on it advertises a three-inch bauble as a six-inch one. One relationship covers every case -- row height squared equals radius change squared plus axial rise squared -- and it also explains why "+6 sc per round" makes a flat circle, and tells us when the fabric must gather. | Yes |
| B-060 | 2026-09-17 | The twin refuses a width and a height for a closed shaped piece, and states a circumference instead. | Rounds 2-5 of every amigurumi grow at almost exactly one row height, which is geometrically the flat case, so the surface model sincerely reports a hemisphere as a half-centimetre pancake. It is not wrong about the fabric; the ball is made by the stuffing and the maker's tension, and neither is in the gauge. A circumference is arithmetic and a height is a physical measurement, so we give the first and refuse the second (section 3). | No |
| B-061 | 2026-09-17 | A product's own name is checked against the shape the pattern makes. | "Market Basket Trio" was a single flat rectangle whose designer note admitted it was "the side panel, seamed into the basket", with no seaming instruction in the document; "Hexagon Coaster Set" was a rectangle. Both were certified and drafted into listings. The check is deliberately narrow -- a three-dimensional noun, or a shape word attached to the object -- because mosaic work legitimately depicts stars and hearts *on* rectangles and over-flagging that would block real products. | Yes |
| B-062 | 2026-09-17 | A size claim the twin cannot check is refused rather than ignored. | The claim check skipped any dimension where the twin had no number, which is exactly the case where a claim is least supportable: the piece the twin refuses to measure. "Nothing to compare it against" was being treated as "nothing to object to". | Yes |
| B-063 | 2026-09-17 | Written instructions name the yarn on every row of a multi-colour pattern, and the reverse compiler checks it. | The whole catalogue's written instructions omitted colour: a two-colour mosaic was only workable from the chart. In overlay mosaic the colour *is* the motif, so a document that names the wrong yarn produces a different object, which makes colour a structural check rather than a presentational one. | Yes |
| B-064 | 2026-09-17 | How the rounds are worked is stated once per component and read back from the text. | Joining each round leaves a seam up the side; spiralling does not. A round-worked document that says neither is missing an instruction the maker needs, and one that says the opposite describes a different fabric. Stated once rather than repeated on sixty lines, so there is one source of truth to check. | No |
| B-065 | 2026-09-17 | The finishing is part of the CIR, and a flat panel may be called a basket only once something joins it. | Multi-piece patterns without assembly steps are a bag of rectangles and a wish. Seams name their pieces, the compiler checks those pieces exist, the writer emits the steps and the reverse compiler reads them back -- so the shape claim gate can ask a real question: is there anything here that turns these pieces into that object? | No |
| B-066 | 2026-09-17 | The customer document carries its own version, and re-certification replaces the stored certificate. | The release hash is taken over the CIR *and* the written text, so a change to the writer invalidates every certificate issued before it -- while the PDF quietly regenerates from the new writer. Persisting the release was insert-if-absent, which is the fourth appearance of "code changed, the deployed database did not" (B-027, B-051, B-054). | Yes |
| B-067 | 2026-09-17 | A product whose certificate is refused has its listing withdrawn. | Refusing a certificate and leaving the storefront untouched leaves a draft listing for a product the release chain has just rejected. The only thing between that draft and a customer was shadow mode, and shadow mode is a phase, not a guarantee. | No |
| B-068 | 2026-09-17 | Launch readiness separates blockers into build, integration and owner, and only the owner's reach the owner queue. | The directive forbids asking for an Etsy account, KYC or banking "because they will eventually be needed", and the honest test of that is whether the system can tell the difference between a requirement it has not met yet and one it cannot meet at all. Every unmet requirement names who it waits on; a requirement blocked on build never carries an owner request, and the test suite asserts both directions. | No |
| B-069 | 2026-09-17 | The owner queue is written by the system on a daily cadence, not by hand. | The table has had the directive's five columns -- action, reason, maximum cost, minutes, consequence of delay -- since the first build and nothing had ever written to them, because until the catalogue, imagery, pricing and copy existed every blocker was ours. Now that the remaining blockers really are the owner's, the company says so itself, and re-running the assessment does not duplicate what is already queued: an owner queue that grows by seven a day is a queue nobody reads. | No |
| B-070 | 2026-09-17 | Idempotency keys for design-derived work carry the design's own fingerprint, and the rebuild compares the stored design against the one the code produces. | Found by watching production accept the round-worked rebuild and keep serving the old one. `cir.draft` was keyed `draft:{slug}` -- once per slug, forever -- so a re-engineered product could never enter the chain: the draft was done, the compile key was taken, certification never re-ran, and the `DOC_VERSION` bump meant to deliver the fix could not reach the stage it was bumped for. A slug and a version do not identify a design; the design's content hash does. This is the fifth face of "code changed, the deployed database did not", and the one that broke the previous four fixes' own delivery mechanism. | Yes |
| B-071 | 2026-09-17 | The post-certification chain is keyed on the certified release hash, and a listing records which release produced it. | Found in production ten minutes after B-070, which is the same bug one stage lower: certification re-ran, produced a new release hash, and then found `listing:{slug}:{version}:c{chain}` already taken by the previous rebuild, so the corrected design certified and the old listing stayed. The chain version catches a code change; only the release hash catches a design change, because a re-engineered product keeps its slug and its version. | Yes |
| B-072 | 2026-09-17 | A rebuild's trigger is part of the idempotency key and rides the whole chain. | The third layer of one bug, each layer found by watching production refuse to change. A rebuild exists precisely for the case where the work already ran and its result is no longer right, so keying only on the product, the code version and the certified release still leaves every stage's key taken. The rebuild now mints a token from the transition it is correcting and passes it down the chain, so each stage re-runs once for that rebuild and not once per cadence. | Yes |
| B-073 | 2026-09-17 | The rebuild records why it judged each listing current or stale. | It reported "nothing to do" three times while two products sat visibly wrong in production, and from outside the process that claim was unfalsifiable. The audit entry now carries the comparison it made for every listing. | No |
| B-074 | 2026-09-17 | A physical sample calibrates the yarn estimate and falsifies the size claim, and those are kept separate. | Folding a size disagreement into a calibration factor would turn the one check that can catch a wrong geometry model into a number that silently absorbs it. So a yarn difference within a plausible band becomes a factor; a finished size outside 12% of the claim raises a defect against the product instead. The factor is keyed on the yarn and the stitch it was measured on -- a test caught an earlier version keying on weight alone, where "worsted cotton" and "worsted acrylic" collided. | Yes |
| B-075 | 2026-09-17 | The Etsy publishing path is written and unit-tested while it cannot publish. | It is the only code in the system that could reach a customer, and the alternative is writing it for the first time on the day it matters. Three refusals sit in front of every request, in this order: phase, then the owner's authority, then credentials -- so a misconfigured key can never be the reason we stopped, and a present key is never mistaken for permission. The transport is the only way out and the tests count its calls: in shadow, with credentials present, zero requests are made. | No |
| B-076 | 2026-09-17 | The client refuses a listing it cannot map rather than truncating it. | A title cut at Etsy's 140 characters mid-word is a listing nobody approved, published under a certificate that describes something else. The limits are Etsy's and the policy gate already enforces them upstream, so this check should never fire -- which is exactly why it is here. | No |
| B-077 | 2026-09-17 | "Listing created, file not attached" is reported as its own outcome. | A listing on Etsy with no digital file takes money and delivers nothing. It is neither a success nor a plain failure: the recovery is to complete or delete that listing, not to create another one. It also surfaces the dependency on object storage, because a process that renders a PDF, restarts, and then uploads has nothing to send. | No |
| B-078 | 2026-09-18 | A seam says *where* on the piece it happens, and a multi-piece join that does not is reported. | Knowing that the ear attaches to the head does not make a toy; knowing it attaches across rounds 4 to 6, six stitches either side of centre, does. A pattern that omits placement leaves the maker to reverse-engineer it from the photograph, which is the "beauty image, guess the instructions" failure the Execution Directive forbids, arriving through the back door of an unspecified assembly step. Placement is validated against the piece it attaches to: a join at round 40 of a seven-round head, or two ears that would overlap at the centre, are errors that every other check in the system would pass, because the pieces themselves are correct. | No |
| B-079 | 2026-09-18 | No amigurumi is engineered yet, because the radar has not selected one. | The capability was the gap and the capability is built. | B-083 | 2026-09-18 | The customer PDF renders reproducibly, so its hash means what the code says it means. | Found in production: two renders of one certified release, three minutes apart, audited under two different hashes, because reportlab stamps the current time and a random document id into every render. | B-084 | 2026-09-18 | The container installs the fonts the renderer needs, and a missing font is a blocking problem rather than ugly output. | Every listing image ever rendered in production had a face a few pixels tall, because Pillow's `truetype` raises and its fallback is a bitmap font that ignores the requested size -- silently, successfully, and at a size that makes a title unreadable at thumbnail scale. | B-086 | 2026-09-18 | A hero image whose fabric has no internal contrast is refused, and the check measures the fabric rather than the frame. | My own frame-level thumbnail check passed the blank hero at 78% coverage and 10.6:1 contrast while production blocked the same product at 11% and 1.4:1. So `Frame.fabric_is_flat` asks the fabric itself whether it has at least three shades spanning twelve levels, and `LISTING_HERO_FABRIC_FLAT` blocks the plan. | B-087 | 2026-09-18 | Production reports the commit it is running, and reports `unknown` rather than guessing. | Every other honesty check in this system compares a claim against evidence; "the deploy landed" was the exception, and it was an assumption wearing a fact's clothes. The container has no `.git`, so only the build environment knows the sha -- and when it does not provide one, the honest answer is `unknown`, which never matches any commit. | B-089 | 2026-09-18 | Every square of a multi-colour chart carries its yarn's letter, and a chart too small to carry one is reported rather than shipped. | Master Plan section 31 asks for colour-independent cues where practical, and this was the most expensive kind of gap: everything about the charts was correct and internally consistent, and a maker with a colour vision deficiency still could not read one. | B-094 | 2026-09-18 | A readiness assessment can be started on demand, like the chain rebuild. | The owner queue's wording comes from the assessment and the assessment runs daily, so a deploy that corrects what an action should say leaves the owner reading yesterday's wording until midnight. | B-096 | 2026-09-18 | The shadow cycle is built once and shared by the tests that only read it, with a guard that fires. | Four of nine tests in that file were 60 seconds each, and three of them asserted different things about an identical run -- one piece of evidence paid for three times. | B-099 | 2026-09-18 | A runner that has started and not yet ticked reads as starting, not dead, for a bounded grace derived from its own timings. | Observed in production during this heartbeat: `/api/verify` reported `worker_is_alive` and `scheduler_has_ticked` failing, and the worker was fine -- it had started 85 seconds earlier and the first tick lands at `_START_DELAY` 25s plus `_SCHEDULER_INTERVAL` 60s. | B-100 | 2026-09-18 | The continuity export is portable JSON Lines, not `pg_dump`. | Requirement 51 asks for the company to be reconstructable "if a SaaS provider disappears", and a `pg_dump` restores into Postgres and nowhere else -- so an engine-native backup satisfies the letter of "we have backups" while preserving the exact dependency the requirement exists to remove. | B-109 | 2026-09-18 | A coverage gap cannot leave the queue without a reason, and cannot skip a lifecycle state. | #314 lists 'not pursuing + reason' as a state rather than an absence, and the reason is the requirement. | B-117 | 2026-09-18 | The benchmark library is quarantined in code, and the quarantine was built before any file existed. | The owner will buy about ten competitor patterns, which is legitimate and ordinary, and it puts a folder of somebody else's copyrighted instructions on the same disk as a system that writes crochet patterns. | B-120 | 2026-09-18 | The Etsy v3 API key is `keystring:shared_secret`, and the owner was right that we had it wrong. | Verified against Etsy's own authentication documentation before changing anything, because taking a technical correction on faith is how the second bug gets written to fix the first. | B-122 | 2026-09-18 | The monthly model ceiling is checked before the call, not reconciled after it. | The owner approved a maximum, and a ceiling enforced after the fact is a report about an overspend. `check()` sums the month's ledgered `llm` cost and refuses a call whose estimate would cross CA$25, which also means every model task must declare a token estimate -- an undeclared task cannot be routed, because its cost cannot be checked. | B-123 | 2026-09-18 | The analysis cache is keyed on content plus the routed model. | The owner asked for caching, and the obvious key is the content fingerprint alone -- which is wrong in one specific and flattering direction. | B-124 | 2026-09-18 | An at-risk seasonal window is an incident; a missed one is not. | The daily sentinel could raise on everything late, and within a week nobody would read the channel -- which costs the alert that mattered. | B-125 | 2026-09-18 | The CA$5K probability is a minimum over critical layers, never a weighted average. | #274 says the modelled probability cannot exceed the weakest critical layer, and the reason is a specific failure shape: a company that can design, engineer, photograph, price and schedule beautifully, and cannot get anybody to buy, has six strong layers and one fatal one. The minimum means the only way to raise the number is to fix the thing that is actually broken. | Yes |
| B-126 | 2026-09-18 | Every layer measured from orders is bounded by the order count in the ledger. | Several rungs need counts from systems that do not exist yet -- attribution for acquisition loops, repeat tracking, portfolio roles -- so they are caller-supplied, and that is the hole every scoring model has. Without the bound, a caller could report a conversion sample of ten thousand visits and a perfectly diversified portfolio into a database holding no sales, and the ladder would believe all of it. Conversion, order value, repeat and resilience are all *measured from orders*, and the order count is the one figure that comes from the ledger rather than from an argument, so it bounds them. The only way to raise this model is to sell something. | Yes |
| B-127 | 2026-09-18 | An observed rate is shrunk toward the pessimistic prior by its own sample size. | The alternative is that the first lucky week sets the company's expectations for the year: two visitors and one sale is a 50% conversion rate, and treating it as one produces a plan that needs a tenth of the traffic it really needs. Shrinkage means a rung earns confidence by accumulating observations rather than by getting lucky early, and it degrades gracefully -- a small sample is not rejected, it is simply worth little. Full weight at a thousand visits for conversion and a hundred orders for value, both stated in the evidence rather than buried. | No |
| B-128 | 2026-09-18 | The 75% gate is six counts, and the model is capped at 0.74 until all six exist. | #275 lists what a >=75% claim requires, and a list of prose conditions is a list somebody satisfies by argument. As counts -- four selling SKUs, two families, forty outside customers, sixty orders, two acquisition loops, three months of history -- it is satisfied by rows or not at all. The ceiling is a gate rather than a cap, and that is deliberate: a model that could only ever report near zero would stop being read, and then the honest number would go unseen for the opposite reason. | Yes |
| B-129 | 2026-09-18 | Every revenue scenario states contribution as well as gross. | CA$5,000 gross is about CA$4,430 after Etsy's 6.5% transaction fee, payment processing, listing fees and a modest refund rate -- and the owner banks in CAD while the marketplace prices in USD. A plan that tracks gross alone reaches its number on paper and misses it in the bank, and the gap is eleven percent, which is large enough to matter and small enough to go unnoticed. Required visits are computed only on the new-on-platform share, because repeat buyers and off-platform orders do not arrive through the search funnel: counting them inflates the traffic requirement and makes every path look impossible. | Yes |
| B-130 | 2026-09-18 | Every scheduled cadence is run, not just checked for wiring. | Two cadences died on their first production run in one session -- one built a scratch database the ephemeral-storage guard refused, one constructed an `Incident` with a keyword that model does not have -- and the existing cadence test passed both times, because it proves an agent may run a job type and a handler is registered, which is wiring. A handler that raises on the empty database every first boot starts from is invisible to that check. So each cadence is now enqueued and executed against a seeded database, with exactly one exemption: the shadow-mode publication refusal, which the test asserts must be present rather than merely tolerating, because its absence would mean Gate F had stopped working. | Yes |
| B-131 | 2026-09-18 | A test that can pass without entering the branch it is about is a test that has not been written. | The seasonal sentinel's test asserted over the incident list and passed while the handler raised in production on every single run, because on the day it was written nothing in the fixture was at risk and the list was empty. `all(...)` over an empty sequence is true, which is the specific shape of this mistake. The fix is not a better assertion, it is a fixture that guarantees the branch: the test now picks a date five days inside a real product's at-risk window and asserts exactly one incident with its slug and runway. The handler gained an `as_of` input to make that possible, which an operator can also use to ask what the war room looked like on any day. | Yes |
| B-132 | 2026-09-19 | The observation pipeline is built and tested before the credential exists. | The owner approved the Etsy application and the key is not set yet, and the standing rule is to route around a blocked integration rather than wait on it. The stronger argument is what the alternative costs: a pipeline written the day the key arrives is a pipeline whose first debugging session happens against a live marketplace, where a pagination mistake is a rate-limit ban and an unchanged-detection bug is a bill. Driven by an injected reader, every branch -- baseline, unchanged, new, changed, unreadable gallery, no credential -- is exercised now, and the day the key lands the scan either works or fails for a reason that is about Etsy rather than about us. | Yes |
| B-133 | 2026-09-19 | The change fingerprint covers what moves commercially and deliberately excludes what moves hourly. | Fingerprinting every field Etsy returns is the obvious implementation and it destroys the feature: `views` changes constantly, so every listing would read as changed on every scan, every gallery would be re-opened, and the cost model that makes six-hourly checks affordable would collapse -- while the code still looked like it had change detection. The fields chosen are the ones a buyer would notice: title, description, price, tags, materials, state, last-modified and favourites. The test asserts on the number of gallery calls rather than on the rows, because the rows are identical whether or not this works. | Yes |
| B-134 | 2026-09-19 | An uncovered arena is scored only on what the scan observed, with the missing components left absent. | The gap queue decides where the company's scarcest resource -- finished, certified products -- gets spent, and a scan can genuinely observe demand proxies and portfolio fit while it cannot observe make time, contribution or creative potential. Filling those with midpoints would produce a fuller-looking score that ranks arenas by an opinion, and once in the table an opinion is indistinguishable from a measurement. Leaving them out makes `evidence_weight` read under 0.4, which is the honest description of a ranking built from two components out of seven. | Yes |
| B-135 | 2026-09-19 | Palette is not a term in the concept distance function. | #84 excludes superficial colour and name variations, and the only way to enforce that is to make a recolour score zero. Including palette would let twenty colourways read as a diverse field, the tournament would pass, and every report would show a healthy creative process producing one product. Rewriting the premise cannot rescue an identical structure either: the premise term is capped at 0.15, so a new sentence over the same form, construction, motif, recipient, occasion and function still lands inside the clone radius. | Yes |
| B-136 | 2026-09-19 | Critics may only reject, and one rejection ends the concept. | A scoring jury averages the fatal objection away: five approvals and one "this is our fourth throw" produces a healthy number and the fourth throw ships. Same arithmetic error as averaging the CA$5K confidence ladder, and the same fix. It also makes the autopsy useful -- every death has exactly one named cause, so the aggregate says whether the field has a generation problem or a brief problem, which are different repairs. | Yes |
| B-137 | 2026-09-19 | Structural cleanliness is never approval; the best verdict without a vision model is `needs_taste`. | The structural critics are mechanical and real, and not one of them can tell whether a product is desirable -- that needs eyes. The tempting shortcut is to let a structurally clean concept through to engineering, which is precisely what #83 forbids: engineered because it filled a category slot. So the gate has three verdicts, not two, and the middle one is honest about who has not looked yet. It also means the model credential unlocks a capability that is already wired rather than one that must then be designed. | Yes |
| B-138 | 2026-09-19 | The creative defect is measured from the compiled CIR, not from the concept mapping. | Auditing our own catalogue through the concept vocabulary involves choices -- which slug implies which form, what a missing field defaults to -- and a conclusion resting on those is arguable, which is the last thing a defect report should be. So the audit also compiles all eleven products and reports what came out: every one is a single-component flat-rows panel using exactly two stitches. A garland, an ornament, a wall hanging, a table runner and a baby blanket are the same object at different widths. That finding survives any disagreement about the mapping. | Yes |
| B-139 | 2026-09-19 | A self-improvement hypothesis is refused at proposal time, not reviewed afterwards. | Every number this company reports is produced by a check, so the cheapest way to improve any of them is to loosen the check. "Relax asset truth so more assets pass" raises the pass rate immediately, permanently and measurably, and from inside the loop it is indistinguishable from fixing a defect: a change, then a better number. Reviewing afterwards does not work either, because by then somebody has built the thing and the argument becomes about the work rather than the rule. So the refusal happens before any effort is spent, against named capabilities rather than file paths -- a path blocklist is evaded by a refactor. | Yes |
| B-140 | 2026-09-19 | An undeclared surface is itself a refusal. | The obvious evasion of a protected-gate check is to propose "loosen the validation a little" and declare nothing, leaving the list with nothing to match against. Requiring the touched surfaces to be declared whenever the hypothesis reads as loosening makes the protected list operative rather than advisory, and costs an honest proposal one field. | Yes |
| B-141 | 2026-09-19 | The baseline is captured when the hypothesis is proposed, and a first measurement is not a win. | A result compared against a number taken after the change is compared with itself. Every promotion then succeeds, the capability history rises smoothly, and the loop is decorative -- which is the failure mode of most improvement processes rather than an exotic one. So `propose()` records the baseline and `test_result()` refuses a hypothesis that had none, converting that first measurement into the baseline for next time. It costs one cycle and buys the meaning of every cycle after it. | Yes |
| B-142 | 2026-09-19 | A degrading promotion reverts itself rather than raising an alert. | An alert is a request for somebody's attention, and the period when a promoted change is quietly degrading a metric is exactly the period when nobody is looking -- overnight, over a weekend, during the seasonal run that made the change seem urgent. The rollback reference already exists as a precondition of promotion, so reverting is mechanical. The post-promotion observation is recorded either way, because a revert that erased its own evidence would hide the pattern of what keeps failing. | Yes |
| B-143 | 2026-09-19 | Compounding is measured by lessons acted on, never by lessons recorded. | #101 asks that the company stop starting from generic intelligence, and a lesson count is the metric that makes filing look like learning: it rises every time somebody writes something down and never falls when nothing changes. Acting on a lesson is recorded against the lesson by the cell that acted, a cell that was never routed one cannot claim it, and lessons sitting routed and untouched past thirty days are surfaced as exactly that. | Yes |
| B-144 | 2026-09-19 | Every dashboard block is guarded so one unhappy subsystem cannot take the page down. | The owner asked not to have to infer progress from code logs, and a command centre that returns a stack trace because the seasonal engine raised tells them nothing about the twelve subsystems that are fine -- while failing at exactly the moment they most want to look. Each block renders its own failure in place and the rest of the page survives, which is also how the launch-readiness block already behaved; this generalises it rather than inventing it. | No |
| B-145 | 2026-09-19 | The seasonal calendar rolls, so an event is never discovered late. | #286 objects to one-off strike teams, and the reason is what a shop with them does: Christmas gets attention in October, Easter gets none until March, and the eleven months between are spent finding out that the next window closed during the last event. On a rolling calendar every event is always some number of days away -- Christmas is 364 days out on Boxing Day rather than vanishing -- and the phase its distance implies decides what work is useful today. The phase boundaries come from the lead-time engine's own arithmetic rather than from taste, so the two cannot disagree. | Yes |
| B-146 | 2026-09-19 | A trend's half-life caps the make lane it may justify, as a refusal rather than a penalty. | Ranking a flash trend lower still lets it win when nothing else is queued, and the cost of losing that argument is asymmetric: weeks of flagship engineering on something that expires before a customer could finish it loses the work and the window. A ceiling per half-life is mechanical, and an unclassified trend is refused outright, because "unclassified" is how a trend gets whatever effort somebody felt like spending. | Yes |
| B-147 | 2026-09-19 | Seasonal depth is measured against the departments an event spans, not against a product count. | "Six Christmas products" is the metric that makes a catalogue of five blankets and a coaster look like range. Each event declares the maker-and-gift ecosystem it actually spans -- a Christmas spans nearly everything, a Valentine's does not -- and coverage is the share of those departments with an answer. Measured today: ecosystem depth 0.07, and Christmas has nothing in garments, stockings, ornaments, bags, hats or seasonal gift. | Yes |
| B-148 | 2026-09-19 | A growth loop's strength is derived from recorded traffic, and the CA$5K acquisition rung reads the registry rather than a caller. | Found by a test that broke for the right reason. The registry began as a module constant listing every loop this business could plausibly run, and capping the acquisition rung against it was correct in direction and wrong in substance -- a constant cannot become true, so the rung could never rise however much traffic arrived. Loops are now rows: strength is `untested` until traffic is recorded, `attempted` below the sample floor, `measured` at two hundred visits and `repeatable` only on being seen again. A caller may still pass a number and the rows cap it. Without this, listing seven channels would have raised the modelled probability by writing down ambitions. | Yes |
| B-149 | 2026-09-19 | An experiment's design decides what its result may claim, and pre/post may never claim cause. | #266 asks to distinguish incremental revenue from revenue that would have happened anyway, and the honest implementation is not a caveat in a report -- it is a field on the record. Holdout and staggered rollout support a causal claim; pre/post cannot separate the change from the week it happened in, and in a seasonal gift business the week is frequently the largest effect. So a pre/post result is recorded as association in its own verdict text, where the next person to read it cannot miss it. | Yes |
| B-150 | 2026-09-19 | Thresholds and sample floors are fixed before the experiment runs, and a concluded experiment cannot be reopened. | Choosing afterwards what would have counted as success makes every experiment succeed, and it does not feel like cheating, which is what makes it the common failure rather than an exotic one. The sample floor matters as much: an experiment with none concludes on its first data point, and stopping early at a good number is the most reliable way to find an effect that is not there. Both are refused at construction, and feeding a concluded experiment more data until it says something nicer is refused too. | Yes |
| B-151 | 2026-09-19 | The weekly constraint is ranked by gain per week of effort, not by distance from target. | The largest shortfall is usually the slowest to close -- qualified visits is three weeks of work, conversion is one -- so ranking by gap sends agents confidently to the wrong place. Ranking by expected monthly contribution per week makes the comparison the one that actually decides where a week should go. With every term at zero the solver says something different and more useful than a ranking: the product of zeros cannot be moved by one term, and this is not an optimisation problem yet. | No |
| B-152 | 2026-09-19 | Swarm fan-out is bounded by spend, never by a headcount constant. | #174 forbids artificial scarcity and #188 requires central budget control, and those are one requirement seen twice: the ceiling that matters is money. A constant is wrong in both directions -- too small during a catalogue baseline, and expensively awake on a quiet Tuesday -- and it also hides the real constraint, so nobody learns that the limit was ever financial. Four hundred open items justifies fifty specialists at CA$25 remaining and eight at CA$0.40, and the answer says which bound applied. | Yes |
| B-153 | 2026-09-19 | A priority band is never traded away for value or urgency. | The tempting implementation collapses band, deadline and value into one score, and then a CA$5,000 opportunity with a one-day deadline jumps ahead of a customer whose download is broken. The ordering between bands is a commercial argument -- trust that cannot be re-earned, windows that cannot be reopened -- and the scheduler does not get to re-open it. Deadline and value decide within a band, scaled to stay below one band step. | Yes |
| B-154 | 2026-09-19 | An idle agent takes a standing backlog rather than polling or stopping. | Polling to satisfy a 24/7 claim spends money to produce a heartbeat, and stopping makes the claim false. The third option is the only honest one, and it only works if the backlog is real work ordered by what the business needs -- benchmark change detection first, housekeeping last -- rather than a list of ways to look busy. | No |
| B-155 | 2026-09-19 | Thrash detection keys on the call and the result together. | #34 says three materially identical states without progress. Keying on the call alone would stop a poll whose answer is changing, which is progress; keying on the result alone would stop two different calls that happen to agree. The pair is what distinguishes a loop from work: an agent making the same call and getting the same answer three times has stopped working and started spending, and the answer is to re-plan rather than to wait longer. | Yes |
| B-156 | 2026-09-19 | Nothing becomes the canonical model by being first. | The failure mode is quiet and expensive: generation produces a face, it goes into an asset because it is the only one available, the next asset reuses it for consistency, and a hundred listings later the company has a recurring model nobody chose and cannot change without reshooting everything. So candidates and canonical are separate states with no path between them that does not pass through the owner, `canonical()` returns None today, and replacing an existing canonical identity is refused even with approval -- approving a selection is not approving a redesign. | Yes |
| B-157 | 2026-09-19 | A drift check with no reference pack reports `unavailable`, never `pass`. | This is the same shape as the evidence grading in the benchmark mission, and it matters more here because the failure is invisible: identity drift is gradual, and a buyer notices a gallery where the face keeps changing without being able to say what is wrong. A check that returned `pass` for want of anything to compare against would be believed, and would wave through exactly the assets it exists to catch. An unmeasured dimension counts as maximum drift for the same reason. | Yes |
| B-158 | 2026-09-19 | A gallery frame is judged by the job it was given, and a frame with no job cannot exist. | #78's insight is that a gallery does two different things and one frame cannot do both: the hero creates desire, the later frames substantiate the purchase. Judging every frame against one blended standard produces galleries that are all evidence -- a specification sheet nobody clicks -- or all dream, which converts and then refunds. Declaring the job also makes #80 mechanical: a frame with no commercial job is filler by definition, so it is refused at construction rather than counted toward a target. | Yes |
| B-159 | 2026-09-19 | No rung of the creative escalation ladder lowers the standard, and the last one is to hold the listing. | #77 says the visual standard does not drop because an asset was AI-assisted, and a ladder ending in "accept the best available" would quietly contradict it every time the tooling was weak. The rungs go cheap to expensive -- tighter brief, new composition, different tool, deterministic render, physical sample -- and then stop. Holding a listing is a real outcome rather than a failure of the process: a listing held is a listing that did not lie. | Yes |
| B-160 | 2026-09-19 | A size a motif cannot tile is refused, never rounded. | Found the first time the grader was run against plausible numbers: a twelve-stitch motif at 16 sts/10cm cannot express a 2XL within two centimetres of its target. That combination is the worst available, because nothing catches it before a customer does. The refusal names all three honest fixes (narrower motif, different gauge, fewer sizes) so it is actionable rather than merely negative; four-, six- and eight-stitch motifs all close the same run. | Yes |
| B-161 | 2026-09-19 | A graded size run must be monotonic in every dimension. | One transposed number in a size table gives a 2XL narrower shoulders than an XL, and every count in it is internally consistent -- the pattern compiles, the twin agrees, the document is correct, and the garment is wrong. | B-162 | 2026-09-19 | Ease is declared per size rather than scaled with the body. | Scaling ease is the intuitive implementation and it changes the fit as the run grows: five centimetres is generous on an XS and negligible on a 3XL, so a constant proportion produces a small that is loose and a large that is tight while the table looks consistent. Declaring it per size makes the fit a design decision at every point in the run, and negative ease -- a garment smaller than the body -- is refused outright rather than treated as negative design intent. | Yes |
| B-163 | 2026-09-19 | A teardown audit is complete or it is not scored. | The same rule the twelve-dimension scorecard already applies to its dimensions, pushed down to the elements inside one. Scoring five of twelve chart elements and reporting the mean lets a teardown that looked at the easy things outrank one that looked at all twelve, and the mean is the number that gets quoted. Since ten purchases times nine audits is ninety schedules, the pressure to answer the quick half is real and constant -- which is exactly why it is a refusal rather than a convention. | Yes |
| B-164 | 2026-09-19 | Only the extreme scores owe a mechanism. | The opposite rule -- a justification per element -- is the more rigorous-looking one and it is why this work would never get done: 92 elements per product, ten products. A 4 or 5 is a mechanism worth adopting and a 0-2 is a trap worth making impossible, and those are refused without one. The same trade #170 makes at intake: ask for what cannot be inferred, and nothing else. | Yes |
| B-165 | 2026-09-19 | A benchmark strength with no Brambleloop answer is recorded as unmatched, never converted into a requirement. | #163 refuses parity as a position, and the mechanical version of that is what a strong element becomes: a requirement to *exceed* it, naming the advantage that does. When nobody can name one, the honest output is "a competitor does something we have no answer to" -- which is the single most valuable thing a teardown produces, and the first thing an invented differentiator destroys. A row that looks finished is worth less than a row that is true. | Yes |
| B-166 | 2026-09-19 | An audit the product never promised is refused, not scored zero. | A pattern sold without video does not have bad video support; it has none, and a zero on the video dimension would make a category difference look like a defect. | B-167 | 2026-09-19 | A teardown finding enters the Improvement Department through `improve.governance`, never beside it. | "Their listing states a size range the document does not grade all the way through" is a true, useful observation and a forbidden hypothesis, and only the boundary can tell the difference. | B-168 | 2026-09-19 | An unrun pre-launch benchmark challenge blocks the release. | Same shape as B-157 and for the same reason: a gate that passes when its evidence is missing is not a gate, and the moment it matters most is the moment the evidence is thin. #168 exists to catch us shipping something a buyer would rate below what they already own, so "no benchmark purchased yet" cannot be the state in which it waves things through. It is carried in launch readiness as a requirement with an owner request attached, so the block is visible and has a named way out. | Yes |
| B-169 | 2026-09-19 | Portfolio *performance* and portfolio *shape* are separate modules. | Recorded because this build got it wrong: `growth/portfolio.py` was overwritten rather than extended by the CA$5K work, which would have broken the weekly `portfolio.review` cadence on its next run -- caught by the guard test before production reached it. | B-170 | 2026-09-19 | An export declares its table list in the header and its completion in a trailer. | Per-table row counts caught a file cut mid-table and missed a file cut exactly on a table boundary -- every table present was internally consistent, so every count agreed and the restore reported success with tables silently absent. | B-171 | 2026-09-19 | A check that only runs in the failure state must be tested in the failure state. | `/api/verify` compared an aware cutoff against naive SQLite datetimes, inside a comprehension that only executes when the dead-letter list is non-empty.
