| B-172 | 2026-09-19 | The culture engine's rights router was built before anything else in the package. | A culture radar is the most dangerous thing a pattern company can own, and the danger does not arrive as a decision. It arrives as a gradient: the radar optimises for demand, the strongest signals are all somebody's property, and each step downstream receives a slightly more abstract description of the same protected thing until it reaches production having been refused by nobody. Nobody chose it. So the router exists before the radar that would feed it, in the same order the benchmark library's quarantine was built before the files arrived. | Yes |
| B-173 | 2026-09-19 | Unclear rights route to the original-concept lane, never to a refusal of the opportunity. | #135 requires this and it is also the only version that survives contact with a deadline. Killing an opportunity because its obvious execution is protected teaches every downstream step that the rights gate is an obstacle, and a gate people argue with eventually loses one of those arguments. Routing to the original lane teaches them the gate is a turning -- and the turning is where a product this company owns comes from, which is #146's whole objective. | Yes |
| B-174 | 2026-09-19 | A decomposition is checked for the protected token, never trusted to have dropped it. | The protected element is the most salient thing in the input, so it is exactly what survives when the decomposition is done by judgement -- "the warmth of watching Snugglepuff" is a sincere attempt at an emotion primitive and it is the infringement intact. `check_free_of()` turns #134's instruction into a property, with word-boundary matching rather than substring, because a check that fires on "art" inside "heart" is one everybody learns to work around. | Yes |
| B-175 | 2026-09-19 | The public-domain screen refuses to compute a term from a year. | Copyright term depends on authorship, work type, renewal and jurisdiction, and a function that returned True for 1975 would be giving legal advice in the voice of arithmetic -- which is worse than no screen, because arithmetic is believed. The screen passes only works published before 1900, far earlier than any jurisdiction requires, states in the recorded evidence that passing is not clearance, and sends everything else to the original lane or to the owner. | Yes |
| B-176 | 2026-09-19 | Rights feasibility and the make-time window are gates in the opportunity score, not weights. | The same minimum-not-average rule the confidence ladder and the creative jury use, applied where the incentive to average is strongest: a signal scoring five out of five on nine commercial dimensions and zero on rights arithmetically averages to a strong opportunity, and it is a lawsuit with good margins. They multiply rather than add, and a gate that was never observed fails -- "we did not check whether we may sell this" is not a better position than "we may not sell this". | Yes |
| B-177 | 2026-09-19 | Eras and themes are a closed vocabulary; one invented per signal is refused. | An era's entire value is that it is reusable, recurs annually and belongs to nobody: retro ski lodge is available every winter forever and no rights holder can withdraw it in January. An era invented to fit one signal has none of those properties -- it is a franchise with the name filed off, and it produces the dependence #146 exists to end while looking like the cure for it. | Yes |
| B-178 | 2026-09-19 | Tournament breadth is families and distinct premises, never a count. | Fifteen ideas in two families is one idea in fifteen colours, and ten ideas whose premises restate each other is one idea typed ten times. Neither is visible to the creative jury, which reads concepts one at a time and would pass all fifteen: it is a property of the set, so it is checked where the set exists (#138). | Yes |
| B-179 | 2026-09-19 | Any one exit condition closes a cultural window, and unmeasured is not failure. | A trend dies several different ways -- interest decays, copycats arrive, conversion disappoints, the make-time window shuts -- and requiring all four is how capacity stays committed to last month's wave while the next one passes. The symmetric error is treating absent data as bad data: this company has no conversion figures at all, and an exit check that read `None` as failure would exit every trend on its first day. | Yes |
| B-180 | 2026-09-19 | The rapid-response cell's permissions are two closed lists, not a principle. | #141 says speed comes from prioritization and parallelism rather than lower standards, and a principle under deadline pressure becomes a conversation about what counts as lowering one. The pressure also runs exactly backwards from how it feels: the cell exists because a window is closing, so the argument for skipping a gate is strongest precisely when the consequence is worst -- a product rushed into a closing window has the least time left for a correction round. An unnamed gate is refused too, because a gate that can be skipped without the skip being named is already gone. | Yes |

| B-181 | 2026-09-19 | A platform policy is a dated snapshot, never a constant in the code. | A rule encoded in code is true on the day it is written and quietly becomes a claim about the past. The moment that matters is a suspension notice about listings that were compliant when they were created, and by then nobody can say which version of the rules they were checked against. Five sources are watched separately rather than as one "Etsy policy" blob, because each blocks a different workflow and a single blob blocks everything or nothing. | Yes |
| B-182 | 2026-09-19 | Never checked and unchanged are opposite states, and the watch says which. | The natural implementation reports "no material changes" from an empty table, which is the most confident possible way to be wrong. An unread source blocks the workflows it governs and blocks enabling any new asset or product class, which is #35's own requirement read literally: a new class is exactly when the old reading is least likely to cover the case. | Yes |
| B-183 | 2026-09-19 | A material policy change is a digest difference, not a judgement. | "Does this change matter?" is the judgement somebody makes quickly at the end of a week, and the answer is almost always no. Comparing digests against the previous snapshot of the same source means the question reaching a person is "this changed, does it matter" rather than "has anything changed" -- a much better question to be asked while tired. | Yes |
| B-184 | 2026-09-19 | Disclosure and misrepresentation are different problems, and a label does not convert one into the other. | The spec says never assume an AI-generated lifestyle image satisfies Etsy's listing-image requirements, and the seductive reading is that disclosure fixes it. It does not: an AI render of a finished blanket is a picture of a blanket that does not exist, and the buyer is looking at it precisely to find out what they are buying. So asset roles carry a requirement -- photograph or generated-permitted -- and a generated image in a photograph role is refused however carefully it is labelled. A deterministic render of our own chart is the exception, because it is the artefact being sold rather than a depiction of one. | Yes |
| B-185 | 2026-09-19 | Customer-use terms are five deliberate choices from closed options, rendered once. | Two ordinary failures, both prevented mechanically. The terms were never decided -- "do not redistribute" appears because it appears on everything, and then the question a customer actually has (may I sell what I make at a craft fair?) is answered for the first time in a support reply, which becomes the terms. And they drift: PDF, listing and FAQ written at different times by different parts of the system, where a buyer who finds the discrepancy may rely on the most favourable one. So the options are closed, the surfaces render from one decision, and a consistency check refuses divergence. | Yes |
| B-186 | 2026-09-19 | Decided is not enforceable, and the terms say so until somebody qualified has looked. | These are terms this company offers. Whether any of them binds anybody is a legal question, and the honest word until it is answered is "stated". `enforceable` stays False, the rendered PDF says the terms have not been through legal review, and recording a review requires naming who reviewed it and what they covered -- otherwise it is the same unreviewed terms with a more confident label. | Yes |
| B-187 | 2026-09-19 | The policy watch cadence does not fetch. | No policy reader is connected. A cadence that failed on every run because a dependency is absent would be a dead letter with a schedule, which this build has already produced twice. What does not need the network is noticing that a reading is stale or missing and opening a blocking incident for the workflows it governs -- once per source, because a blocking incident re-raised every six hours is an alert people filter, which is no alert with more rows. | Yes |

| B-188 | 2026-09-19 | A price comparison across a confounder is refused, not flagged. | The natural implementation attaches a warning and returns the number anyway. That warning is read by the person who wrote it and by nobody afterwards; the number travels on alone and arrives somewhere it is treated as a measurement. The confounders -- season, traffic source, category, sale state -- are columns rather than notes for the same reason: "was this price or was it December" has to be a filter, and a note cannot be filtered on. | Yes |
| B-189 | 2026-09-19 | An unmeasured elasticity is reported as unmeasured, never as zero. | Zero is not the absence of a claim. It is the specific claim that price does not matter, which is among the most expensive things this system could accidentally say, and it would be said by the most obvious default in the code. The same reasoning as the empty-portfolio concentration and the unrun benchmark challenge: absent evidence has its own value and it is not the neutral one. | Yes |
| B-190 | 2026-09-19 | Incrementality is measured against what the control predicts for the promoted arm's traffic. | Counting the control's own performance as a result of the spend is the entire error, and it makes every ad budget look self-funding. With one arm it cannot be done at all -- a conversion rate measured on paid traffic is a statement about who was shown the listing, and scaling on it buys more of that audience and none of the improvement. So a single arm returns "not separable" rather than a number. | Yes |
| B-191 | 2026-09-19 | A bundle is judged on contribution, and cannot be judged without a pre-bundle baseline. | The failure mode is not that the bundle fails; it is that it succeeds with buyers who were already ours and used to buy the higher-margin thing beside it. Both halves are true at once -- the bundle sold, the buyer chose it -- so nothing looks wrong, units rise and contribution is flat. Revenue is the metric that hides it and every other dashboard rewards busier, which is why the amplification check is separate and explicit rather than a number among nine that all went up. | Yes |
| B-192 | 2026-09-19 | Clicks with no saves is a product problem; saves with no orders is an offer problem. | #47's missing rung, and the two look identical in every aggregate -- both are clicks that did not become orders. The interventions are opposite: reworking price and trust on a product nobody wanted produces a cheaper version of something nobody wants, and the listing has already done its job by getting them there. Checked before the conversion stage so the cheaper diagnosis cannot claim the case first. | Yes |
| B-193 | 2026-09-19 | The class list lives in the module, and the ladder test derives from it. | Found while adding APPEAL_PROBLEM: the test enumerated classes by hand, so a new class with no intervention ladder would have passed. A hand-written list in a test passes unchanged in exactly the situation the test exists for. | Yes |

| B-194 | 2026-09-19 | The reinvestment recommendation is the envelope scaled by the modelled confidence. | The envelope is what survived the reserves; the recommendation is what the evidence supports spending out of it, and those are different numbers. Cash from an unrepeatable source is not evidence that spending will repeat it, so a company with money in the account and no customers recommends nothing -- which is the correct answer and not the one an optimistic CFO would produce. Confidence is read from the ladder rather than accepted as an argument, so a recommendation cannot be manufactured by supplying a flattering number alongside the cash. | Yes |
| B-195 | 2026-09-19 | No code path in the reinvestment module returns owner_approval_required False. | An authority boundary described in a docstring is one the next change forgets. Making every branch return True means the only way to spend is for a person to act on the recommendation, and it means the boundary cannot be eroded by an edge case somebody adds for a small amount. Small amounts are how standing budgets start. | Yes |
| B-196 | 2026-09-19 | There is no function that returns a release count without its four companions. | #52 says track releases/week only alongside defect rate, support burden, conversion and contribution, and the way that requirement is normally satisfied is a dashboard that shows all five and a function that returns one. An agent optimising its own throughput moves the number it can move without a customer, and releases per week is the cheapest of all of them. The pairing is therefore structural rather than presentational. | Yes |
| B-197 | 2026-09-19 | A companion metric moving off zero is a degradation, not an unmeasurable. | Found immediately on running the comparison: the percentage-change guard skipped every metric whose previous value was zero, which drops the single most important movement it can see -- a defect rate going from none to some. The obvious defensive `if not before: continue` was exactly wrong for the lower-is-better metrics. | Yes |
| B-198 | 2026-09-19 | A weekly review that stops nothing must say why, and is not blocked. | A hard requirement to stop something every week manufactures removals, which is worse than the bureaucracy it prevents. A silent empty list is what a bureaucracy reports every week for years. One sentence is the difference: it costs a lean week nothing and it makes an accumulating one visible. | Yes |
| B-199 | 2026-09-19 | The confidence ladder opens with an architecture rung that is earned and changes nothing. | #55 asks for confidence in stages rather than a jump from zero to a modelled band, and a ladder whose every rung reads 0.00 shows no stages at all -- it teaches an owner to stop reading it, which is the opposite of what an honest number is for. Architecture is the one layer this company can honestly earn today, it is derived from rows (a certified pattern, a proved restore, a refused publish, completed work, no accumulating dead letters), and it is non-critical, so earning it does not move the modelled probability by a point. Progress is visible and is not convertible into confidence about revenue. | Yes |
| B-200 | 2026-09-19 | CA$3,000 and CA$5,000 are separate questions, and today they have the same answer. | A model that scaled confidence with the size of the goal would report CA$3,000 as comfortably likely while no customer had ever existed. Both bands are computed independently and both report zero, because the binding layer counts customers: a smaller target is not a nearer one when nothing has been sold. Reporting them side by side makes that visible rather than leaving it to be assumed either way. | Yes |

| B-201 | 2026-09-19 | Contribution per visitor is the first-class metric; conversion rate is an input to it. | #24's example is worth restating because every instinct disagrees with it: a CA$25 pattern converting at 1.6% beats a CA$12 pattern at 2.5%, and the second listing looks healthier on every dashboard. Conversion rate is a ratio whose denominator the company is also buying, so optimising it optimises the wrong half. The ranking function reports explicitly when the two orderings disagree, because that disagreement is the requirement rather than a footnote to it. | Yes |
| B-202 | 2026-09-19 | Required visits are derived only from an observed conversion rate, never from a category benchmark. | The benchmark is available, plausible and would make the funnel complete -- which is exactly the problem. A funnel built from category numbers produces a confident plan about a company that does not exist, and it is indistinguishable in shape from a plan built from evidence. The arithmetic that does not need a rate (orders at a price) is still shown, so the honest half is not lost with the dishonest one. | Yes |
| B-203 | 2026-09-19 | The binding constraint is walked in funnel order, and the CTR check precedes the traffic check. | Caught by a test that expected "thumbnail" and got "traffic": a listing with 100,000 impressions and 200 visits satisfies "strong conversion, low traffic" and the correct action is not more impressions. Buying traffic when 99.8% of what already arrives bounces is buying more of a broken funnel, and "low traffic" is the diagnosis that would send us there. Funnel order was the stated principle and the first implementation contradicted it. | Yes |
| B-204 | 2026-09-19 | Allowable CAC is set against contribution, never revenue. | Setting it against the top line is how a business buys customers at a loss while every headline number rises -- the same failure as the bundle judged on revenue, arriving through the acquisition side instead of the catalogue side. | Yes |
| B-205 | 2026-09-19 | #27's qualitative conditions sit beside the counts, and an unstated condition is unmet. | Counts and conditions fail differently: volume arrives from one lucky listing, and the conditions are statements about the shape of the business. A shop with sixty orders from a single viral outlier, a negative contribution margin and an open policy warning satisfies every count in the gate and none of the conditions. Treating an undeclared condition as unknown rather than unmet would let the gate be passed by omission, which is the cheapest way to pass any gate. | Yes |
| B-206 | 2026-09-19 | An artefact produced by uncosted work is reported as a floor, not as cheap. | The averaging instinct is to spread unattributed spend over the artefacts, which makes every unit cost look plausible and the total look understood. An artefact produced by work nobody costed looks free, and free is the most dangerous price -- so the count of uncosted producers is reported per artefact and the unattributed spend is reported as its own number. | Yes |

| B-207 | 2026-09-19 | The brief carries the novelty, not the validator. | The measured defect is that every product in the catalogue compiles to one component shape in two stitches, and no amount of judging fixes it: given a season and a category, the highest-probability output is the commodity, because the commodity is what the category is made of. A validator can only reject what arrives. So the invention matrix, the transformation patterns, the motif grammar and the wow requirement all act on the *brief* -- the generator is asked for a specific unusual thing rather than for a good thing. | Yes |
| B-208 | 2026-09-19 | A brief built from one dimension is refused, and so is one built from two near-synonyms. | One dimension is a category, and a category is what everybody else is already making -- "a Christmas ornament" is not a premise. Near-synonyms are the same failure with two names: storage crossed with organization reads like a combination and is one idea stated twice, which is exactly what a system under pressure to produce pairings would emit. | Yes |
| B-209 | 2026-09-19 | #108's sentence is enforced by removing the title's words from the premise. | "A concept that needs its title to explain why it is interesting is weak" is unenforceable while the two are read together, because the title supplies the interest and the reader cannot tell which half carried it. Deleting the title's words and counting what specific language remains makes the test mechanical, and "Gingerbread Village Basket" / "a gingerbread village basket" leaves nothing. | Yes |
| B-210 | 2026-09-19 | An emotional promise names the physical thing that delivers it, and copy language is refused. | "Cosy" achieved by writing cosy in the listing is the failure #109 exists to name, and it is the cheapest way to satisfy any check that reads text. Requiring an execution -- structure, texture, motif, colour relationship, scale or finish -- means the promise has to survive a buyer looking at the photograph with the sound off. | Yes |
| B-211 | 2026-09-19 | A flagship's WOW mechanism must be grounded in the specific object. | "Clean and correct is not enough" is unenforceable as a sentiment and trivial as a checkbox, and a checkbox is what a generator learns to tick. Naming the mechanism from a closed list is half of it; requiring four specific words about *this* object is the half that stops "it has a reveal" counting. | Yes |
| B-212 | 2026-09-19 | The creative floor is the trailing median, with an absolute floor underneath it. | A fixed threshold is one the company grows past and keeps meeting: six months of learning produces better concepts, the bar that rejected two thirds now rejects none, and the pass rate improving looks like success. A trailing median alone has the mirror failure -- a bad quarter would lower the standard permanently -- so the absolute floor sits underneath. That is a fixed threshold arriving from the other direction, which is acceptable as a floor and not as a bar. | Yes |
| B-213 | 2026-09-19 | Boring is a verdict, and no correctness score overrides it. | #129's most important sentence is that agents must be able to reject technically valid ideas. Every other gate in this system is a correctness gate, and a concept can pass all of them and be the ninth version of something nobody remembers -- the correctness score is precisely what a boring concept passes. So the taste rejection is a closed vocabulary with no numeric content, and it ends the question rather than contributing to a total. | Yes |
| B-214 | 2026-09-19 | A rejection reason is a closed vocabulary, or forty autopsies have forty reasons and no pattern. | The useful output of a tournament that rejected forty concepts is the pattern in the forty. Free text produces forty individually reasonable sentences and no aggregate, so five of six rejections being "obvious" -- which is a brief problem rather than six concept problems -- stays invisible. | Yes |

| B-215 | 2026-09-19 | A brief is generated against a cell, and coverage is measured in cells. | "Christmas crochet patterns" produces what the phrase suggests. The same December contains a mantel stocking, a teacher gift under fifteen dollars, a nursery keepsake kept for thirty years, a table setting and a front door -- different products for different people. A catalogue ends up with six variations on a blanket because nothing ever asked which cell each one was for, and a product count cannot see it: six products against 288 cells reads as six products. | Yes |
| B-216 | 2026-09-19 | Skill level is market segmentation, not difficulty. | A wave of only flagship work has no beginner entry and no impulse purchase; one of only quick makes has nothing anybody keeps. Both look busy, which is why this is measured as shares against a healthy range rather than left to judgement -- and the useful signal is "there is no beginner product at all" rather than "beginners are 22%". | Yes |
| B-217 | 2026-09-19 | Tournament diversity is measured per axis, because the entrant count is what hides its absence. | Twelve concepts sharing one construction have explored one construction, and twelve is exactly the number that makes it look like exploration. Measuring dominance per axis -- form, function, tone, construction, motif family, recipient, make time -- means breadth in one axis cannot stand in for breadth. | Yes |
| B-218 | 2026-09-19 | A crowded archetype needs a named unmet angle before anything is built. | Forty near-interchangeable pumpkin coasters is evidence the archetype sells and evidence the forty-first is invisible, and only the second reading has arithmetic behind it. The instinct runs the other way and is nearly irresistible, so the refusal names the number: "ours will be nicer" is what everybody entering a crowded category believes, including the forty already there. | Yes |
| B-219 | 2026-09-19 | White space is mined from complaints, and an empty complaint set produces nothing rather than a proposal. | A buyer saying what went wrong -- too much sewing, sizing that stops short, a variant nobody makes -- is a product brief with a customer attached, which no search volume report is. With nothing recorded, the tempting fallback is to propose from the category, which is precisely how a white-space agent rediscovers the commodity and reports it as discovery. | Yes |
| B-220 | 2026-09-19 | The mechanism transfers between seasons; the theme does not. | #120's distinction, and the one that collapses first under a deadline, because the finished design is right there and the mechanism needs inventing again. If modular character pockets convert at Halloween, Christmas explores modular character pockets -- not the Halloween product with a hat on it. Enforced by requiring a new theme of its own and by refusing a mechanism named as a product. | Yes |
| B-221 | 2026-09-19 | A missed seasonal milestone is a portfolio failure, and the failure is silent. | A phase that slips does not announce itself; it becomes the next phase, and the first visible symptom is a product that lists in December. Ten dated milestones per occasion make the slip a row rather than a realisation. Run today, Christmas 2026 has five of ten already past -- research, concept freeze, engineering start, physical test and creative production -- with listing/indexing due in a week. | Yes |
| B-222 | 2026-09-19 | The north-star metrics report which of them can be computed today. | Four of the eleven need no customers and seven do. A dashboard showing four numbers and seven blanks is read as four numbers, so the split is stated rather than implied, and the cohort framing is deliberate: a running average of everything ever made moves too slowly to show that anything changed, which is indistinguishable from nothing changing. | Yes |

| B-223 | 2026-09-19 | The collection calendar is checked by the daily sentinel, not by somebody opening a page. | The finding that five of Christmas 2026's ten milestones were already past came from running the function once. A function nobody calls produces that finding once and never again, which is the same as not having it -- and the owner's instruction after the first seasonal finding was explicit: use the engine continuously rather than rediscovering timing manually. One incident for the worst-affected event rather than one per missed milestone, because eight rows about one Christmas is the noise that trains everybody to close the channel. | Yes |

| B-224 | 2026-09-19 | The build loop's graph, queue and parking state live in Postgres, not in a session. | Build 2 did route around the missing Etsy credential -- eight milestones landed while it was unavailable and no requirement waited on it -- but the thing that routed around it was a conversation. The dependency graph was prose in BUILD_STATE, the priority queue was judgement, and the parking decision was made once and remembered by whoever was in the session. All of that evaporates when the session does, and the failure is invisible until the moment it matters. Autonomy that depends on a particular process staying alive is not autonomy. | Yes |
| B-225 | 2026-09-19 | A task may only be parked on a condition the code can test, and an owner-gated requirement with no gate is refused. | Free text would make parking a place to put anything difficult, and a queue that can absorb its own difficulties never reports being blocked and never finishes. The refusal is the load-bearing half: without it an owner-gated requirement with no gate falls through into the ready list as work nobody can do, and the queue reports more ready work than exists -- which is the one number the whole module is for. Running this validator for the first time found four such requirements. | Yes |
| B-226 | 2026-09-19 | Two of the seven owner gates check database rows rather than environment variables. | Benchmark purchases is a BenchmarkProduct row count and advertising authority is a SpendLimit row with a positive cap. Keeping them in the same mechanism as the credential gates is deliberate: the question a gate asks is "is this true yet", not "is there a credential", and a design that only handled credentials would have needed a second, weaker mechanism for everything else the owner does. | Yes |
| B-227 | 2026-09-19 | Never-idle is measured in completed requirements, not in ticks. | A loop that always has something to do is trivially satisfiable by inventing work -- the failure the swarm's standing backlog already names for agents, and it applies with more force to a build loop because nobody is watching. The watchdog therefore asks whether anything has been *completed*, and distinguishes idle-with-ready-work (a stall, an incident) from idle-with-everything-parked (correct, no alarm). Those need opposite responses and look identical from outside. | Yes |
| B-228 | 2026-09-19 | A claim carries a lease, so a dead session loses a worker rather than the build. | The same logic the job queue already uses, for the same reason: a session that dies mid-requirement must not hold it forever. Ninety minutes, after which another worker takes it over with no manual intervention and no human noticing that one was needed. | Yes |
| B-229 | 2026-09-19 | The requirement registry lives inside the package, because the container only copies `src`. | Found by deploying the build endpoint: the registry sat at the repository root, the Dockerfile copies `src`, and so `/api/build2` had been returning 500 in production for as long as it existed while passing every test locally. Moving the data file beside its module makes it travel by construction rather than by somebody remembering a COPY line. The general guard is the more valuable half -- a test that walks the app's declared GET routes and calls each one, so a new endpoint is covered the moment it is registered. | Yes |

| B-230 | 2026-09-19 | The off-device proof counts the same dead letters its verdict tests. | Found by running it against production: the report said sixteen and the condition meant one. Shadow-mode publish refusals are the gate working, so they are excluded from the test -- and they were not excluded from the count printed beside it. A number that does not measure what the verdict beside it measures is the same class of defect as an unmeasured rate reported as zero, and it was doing it in the one report whose whole job is to be believed. | Yes |
| B-231 | 2026-09-19 | A third content boundary, drawn in a different place: generic finished-object characteristics may be preserved. | The teardown library refuses a competitor's instructions; the culture router refuses their property; this one has to permit "cropped V-neck button cardigan" while refusing "their stripe sequence". The permitted side is wide on purpose, because a rule that forbade entering a proven arena would make the intelligence mission conclude that the useful answer is never to act on what it found -- which is the most expensive possible conclusion for the company's stated top priority. Closed list of characteristics, and a refusal for anything that only makes sense as a reference to one shop's product. | Yes |
| B-232 | 2026-09-19 | "MUST consider" is satisfied by a recorded refusal and never by silence. | An obligation nobody can check is a sentence, and silence is exactly what a queue produces when nothing forces a row to exist. A declined arena with a reason somebody can disagree with later is a real answer; no row is not. Entering also requires a differentiator, which is #163's anti-parity rule arriving through the competitive door rather than the teardown one. | Yes |
| B-233 | 2026-09-19 | Seasonalising enumerates eight lenses, and a palette-only answer is reported as a recolour. | An agent told to "seasonalise" answers for the colour and stops, and a palette answer alone is precisely the output this build measured as its creativity defect. Naming the lenses individually -- styling, trim, motif, gift context, accessories, bundles, merchandising -- is what stops the easy one standing in for the question. | Yes |
| B-234 | 2026-09-19 | Every pipeline stage names its gate, and a stage cannot run before its predecessor. | A long pipeline never fails by collapsing; it fails by one stage quietly becoming optional, and a stage nobody named cannot be noticed missing. A failed gate stops the run rather than marking it amber, because amber is how a stage becomes optional in practice. | Yes |

| B-235 | 2026-09-19 | A completion is a transition this system observed, not a status it found on first sight. | Two bugs in one place, both found by watching production. Registry-driven completions -- the commonest kind, because a session finishes work and moves the status -- were recorded only as a sync event, so the watchdog reported a stalled loop while six requirements had just closed. A false alarm in the channel that exists to catch a real one is worse than no channel. Fixing that naively then made the first sync count the whole 140-requirement backlog as completions, and a table created ten seconds ago reported a busy loop. Only a transition counts. | Yes |

| B-236 | 2026-09-19 | The benchmark mission's capacity floor is reserved, not allocated. | Capacity merely allocated to a mission gets borrowed, and the borrowing decision is locally sensible every single time: generic research is busy now, the specialists are idle now, moving them is obviously right. It is obviously right often enough that the mission ends up with an org chart and no throughput, and nobody ever decided to defund it. The refusal names the five kinds of work it would starve, so the cost of borrowing is visible at the moment somebody tries. Above the floor the ceiling is money rather than headcount, which is #302 and #188 being the same requirement seen twice. | Yes |
| B-237 | 2026-09-19 | The catalogue map records judgement columns as absent with the capability named, never inferred from the title. | Silhouette, merchandising mechanism and styling are judgements about a photograph. Inferring them from a listing title would make every row look complete and be fiction -- and *persuasive* fiction, because seven of the ten columns would be right. Completeness is therefore scored against what the available capabilities can observe rather than against all ten, or a correctly limited map would report itself permanently broken. Palette is distinguished from both: it is published per-image by Etsy, so its absence is a request nobody made rather than a capability nobody has. | Yes |
| B-238 | 2026-09-19 | A map that does not know how stale it is is not living. | A catalogue map nobody has refreshed is indistinguishable from a current one right up to the moment it is wrong -- and it is wrong exactly when the shop changed something, which is the only moment anybody is reading it. Every row carries its evidence date and the map reports its oldest. | Yes |
| B-239 | 2026-09-19 | Only a recorded outcome moves a pod's learning memory; repetition is refused as evidence. | The failure every learning system has is confidence that grows with repetition. Forty observations of one shop's Christmas listings is one observation made forty times, and the interpretation built on it feels increasingly certain while nothing has been learned. So the outcome vocabulary is closed -- launches, conversion, complaints, owner vetoes, benchmark confirmations -- and 'observed again' is refused by name, with the reason attached. | Yes |
| B-240 | 2026-09-19 | A challenger wins on outcomes or it does not win, and a tie leaves the incumbent standing. | Promoting on equal evidence is promoting on novelty, which makes the memory track whoever last wrote a paragraph. A superseded lesson is kept rather than deleted: the useful question next year is not what the pod believes but what it used to believe and what changed its mind, and a memory that has only ever been right is a memory that is not learning. | Yes |
| B-241 | 2026-09-19 | The competitor-expression boundary is called, not copied. | The pods' check lived inline inside `lesson()`, and the new learning memory needed the same rule. Extracting it was the only honest option: a boundary that exists twice is a boundary that drifts, and the copy that drifts is always the newer one -- the one written by somebody who did not have the original's reasoning in front of them. | Yes |

| B-242 | 2026-09-19 | Make-time is an interval, and impossibility requires the optimistic bound to have passed. | The owner's correction, and it caught this build making the exact move it refuses everywhere else. The lead-time chain is exact arithmetic over guesses -- seven crochet hours a week, a skill multiplier, no maker ever timed -- and its single date was reported first as a fact and then as an impossibility covering a whole season. Unmeasured is not zero and absent is not inferred; a point estimate is not a proof of impossibility either. Under the interval, LONG Christmas work is high risk rather than closed, and FLAGSHIP is genuinely closed -- so the correction is not an excuse, it is a distinction. | Yes |
| B-243 | 2026-09-19 | The interval is asymmetric and only samples narrow it. | Craft overruns: a project going well finishes a little early, one going badly finishes very late -- frogged rows, a gauge surprise, a fortnight nobody touched it. A symmetric band would be a third wrong claim dressed as rigour. And the only thing that narrows it is a recorded physical test; more planning does not, which is worth stating because planning is what a system under time pressure produces instead. Even fully calibrated it stays an interval, because two people making the same object differ and a tighter band would claim to know the maker rather than the pattern. | Yes |
| B-244 | 2026-09-19 | Executable and ready are two numbers, not one. | An earlier invariant asserted the queue's ready count equalled the registry's executable count. That held only while every gated requirement was also `owner_gated`, and it stopped holding the moment a requirement was half-built with its remainder behind a credential -- the ordinary case rather than the exception. Keeping the equality would have forced a choice between two lies: calling a half-built requirement owner-gated, or calling gated work ready. The honest invariant is that every executable requirement is either ready or parked, and nothing owner-gated is ever ready. | Yes |

| B-245 | 2026-09-19 | A tester's photograph without recorded consent is refused, by the same mechanism as a competitor's. | The error arrives from the friendly direction: the tester was pleased, they posted it themselves, nobody would mind. That is exactly why it needs a mechanical check rather than good intentions -- the competitor case is obvious and this one is not, and they are the same act. A physical photograph also records the yarn it was made in, because a buyer choosing a substitute is choosing against that picture. | Yes |
| B-246 | 2026-09-19 | The digital-not-finished disclosure belongs in the title, not the description. | A buyer who thinks they are purchasing a blanket does not read the description, and that refund is the single most preventable one in the category. "It says so in the description" is true of a description nobody read, which is why the six purchase-protecting disclosures are a closed set checked against the surface each appears on. Confusion contacts are counted as a listing defect rather than a cost of doing business: counting them as a cost budgets for them. | Yes |
| B-247 | 2026-09-19 | The order-to-version map is written at sale time because it cannot be reconstructed. | Once a listing moves to 1.2.0 there is no record of who bought 1.1.0 unless somebody wrote it down at the time. A material correction then cannot say which buyers are affected, and the answer is either a message to everybody or a message to nobody. This is the one requirement here that is impossible to retrofit, which is why it exists before the first order does. | Yes |
| B-248 | 2026-09-19 | A trend datum with no population or window is refused; a foreign one is discounted. | Refusal and discount are different answers to different problems. A number that cannot say whom it measured is not weak evidence about anybody, and discounting it would imply it was. A US crochet figure is genuinely evidence about Canada -- weaker, not worthless -- so it carries a factor, and the factor is baked into the value the consumer sees rather than offered as a caveat beside it, because a caveat is read once and the figure travels on alone. | Yes |
| B-249 | 2026-09-19 | Staleness is measured against the topic's own seasonality. | A Christmas figure from last January is one cycle old and about to matter again; a two-week meme from last January is archaeology. A plain age cut-off treats them identically, and whichever threshold it picks is wrong for one of them. | Yes |
| B-250 | 2026-09-19 | Dependency impact is graded by what stops, and a dependency with no recovery is refused. | Vendor prestige gets this backwards routinely: Postgres holds the company's entire memory and GitHub holds a copy of code that is also on disk and in the running container. One is fatal and one is an inconvenience. The refusal matters more than the grading -- a hand-written map marks three things critical, and those are the three somebody was already worried about rather than the three that would end the company. | Yes |

| B-251 | 2026-09-19 | A challenger is judged on a fixed shared task set, never on tasks it introduced. | The failure that makes every other check in a league decorative. The author of a new configuration has necessarily been thinking about the cases it handles well, so a challenger allowed to bring its own tasks wins whatever the thresholds are -- and the result looks like evidence. Adding a task is a separate act that applies to everybody retrospectively. | Yes |
| B-252 | 2026-09-19 | Cost and reliability are outcomes in the league, and the cost bar scales with the ratio. | A league reporting only quality takes every expensive trade and finds the bill at the end of the month, and a configuration that wins when it answers while failing twice as often has not won. A flat cost threshold was the first implementation and it treats 1.3x and 5x as the same trade: doubling the spend may buy a real improvement, quintupling it has to buy a large one, so the required quality gain scales with the ratio. | Yes |
| B-253 | 2026-09-19 | The weakening detector matches a verb governing a gate object by proximity, not a literal phrase. | Found by writing "lower the thumbnail threshold": the phrase list caught "lower the threshold on the thumbnail check" and missed the same sentence reordered. A detector a paraphrase defeats fails exactly when somebody is rewriting a hypothesis to get it through, which is the only occasion it matters. The first fix -- any weakening verb plus any gate object in one sentence -- then refused "tightening the thumbnail check should reduce defects", a hypothesis that *strengthens* a gate. So the verb has to govern the object, judged by proximity, and both failures now have regression tests. | Yes |

| B-254 | 2026-09-19 | Testing demand is forecast from the seasonal calendar, not from the request queue. | Testing is the one constraint where wanting it faster changes nothing -- a tester crochets at a human speed -- which makes it the one discovered late. A queue of test requests tells you what has already been asked for, and the entire lead time lives in the difference between that and what the calendar says is coming. Reliability is completed against accepted, not enthusiasm: a tester who has not finished anything is not capacity, and one who has never been asked is neither reliable nor unreliable, which the report distinguishes. | Yes |
| B-255 | 2026-09-19 | A specialty covered by one tester is a single point of failure and counts as one. | A network of four people where one does every garment is a network of one for garments, and it reads as four on any headcount. The alarm is per specialty for that reason rather than per network. | Yes |
| B-256 | 2026-09-19 | The brand moat is an inventory with replication time, and the model is the most copyable entry in it. | #44's point is that the recurring model is one asset and not the brand, and the reason it gets mistaken for the brand is that it is the most *visible* asset -- which feels like the same thing as the most valuable. Measuring each signature by how long a competitor would need reframes the list: a naming architecture is an afternoon, version-aware support is structural because the order-to-version map cannot be backfilled. Replication time is what it would cost *them*, never what it cost us: an expensive thing that is easy to copy is a sunk cost. | Yes |
| B-257 | 2026-09-19 | A planned brand signature is not counted as a moat. | Listing an aspiration beside the real ones produces a page saying the company is defensible when it is aspiring, and that page is the one somebody reads before deciding not to build the thing. Five of the ten signatures exist today and the inventory says which five. | Yes |
| B-258 | 2026-09-19 | Realised benefit is never read from the sandbox result that won the promotion. | The promotion records its own result as a capability point, so the naive implementation reads it back as the return and every promotion succeeds by construction -- the most comfortable way for an improvement programme to stop being a measurement. The return is what production measured afterwards, against the baseline captured at proposal time. | Yes |
| B-259 | 2026-09-19 | A promotion younger than the realisation window is too_early, not unmeasured. | Collapsing the two makes a young programme look like a bad one, and a hit rate computed over promotions made this morning is a number about the calendar. Only changes old enough to be readable are counted in the rate. | Yes |
| B-260 | 2026-09-19 | A design that drew on no accumulated lesson is recorded, never refused. | The first product in a new territory legitimately has nothing to stand on. What matters is the catalogue: if every design cites nothing, the company is restarting from generic intelligence each time, which is what #101 is named after -- and that is only visible if the zero-lesson designs are recorded rather than rejected. | Yes |
| B-261 | 2026-09-19 | Family membership requires both that the idea still reads at the new size and that the form can be built that way. | Size compatibility alone pairs an amigurumi robin with a scarf: two small objects, and no amount of increase-and-decrease shaping turns one into the other. Checking only size invents members, which is the forced derivative #111 exists to prevent reached from the other side. A member must also differ from the hero on form, because a family whose members differ by palette is a recolour with a collection name on it. | Yes |
| B-262 | 2026-09-19 | A concept that seeds no family is reported as a single product, not rejected. | A brilliant single product is a legitimate thing to build. It is a bad thing to discover after engineering three derivatives out of it, which is the whole reason the test runs before engineering rather than after. | Yes |
| B-263 | 2026-09-19 | The shopping-window critic judges nothing when nobody said how long is left. | An unknown window is not a comfortable one, and inferring a date would fire the critic on every everyday product. Absent is not inferred, applied at the point where creative capacity is allocated. | Yes |
| B-264 | 2026-09-19 | A department whose forms are all past their launch window is next season's gap, not this season's work. | Spending the runway that is left on something nobody can buy in time is the expensive version of looking busy, and a gap queue that ignores the window produces exactly that. Hurry and stop stay in separate buckets for the same reason they do everywhere else in this build. | Yes |
| B-265 | 2026-09-19 | A brief is never counted as coverage. | A gap report that produces no work is a report, so gaps become briefs -- but a queue that counted its own briefs would report depth the catalogue does not have. Depth moves when something is built, and a test holds the line. | Yes |
| B-266 | 2026-09-19 | The continuity archive is retained in the database, and that is called surviving the container rather than durable. | The proof ran nightly on a file that no longer existed an hour later. Holding the bytes in Postgres survives a replacement, a redeploy and a crash -- the failures that actually happen -- and not the provider disappearing, which is the failure #51 names. The claim stops where the evidence does, and the remaining half is parked on a gate whose condition code can test. | Yes |
| B-267 | 2026-09-19 | An export never contains the previous exports. | Each retained archive is a copy of the export, so including them makes every night's backup carry the compressed bytes of the last three and the backup grows faster than the company it describes. The archive table is excluded, and a test asserts the export size stays flat across four generations. | Yes |
| B-268 | 2026-09-19 | Etsy's policy pages are not fetched by spoofing a browser user-agent. | A plain request returns 403 from their bot protection while robots.txt fetches fine, so the edge is refusing an automated client deliberately. Getting past it by pretending to be a browser is evasion, and doing it to a marketplace this company intends to sell on trades a small convenience for the relationship. The requirement is parked on the browser-worker gate instead. | Yes |
| B-269 | 2026-09-19 | A requirement re-audited as gated is retired from the build queue rather than left as a stale row. | sync() skipped past existing tasks whose requirement stopped being schedulable, so five requirements re-audited this session kept reporting themselves ready. A queue advertising work nobody can start is the exact failure the executor was written to prevent, arriving through its own reconciliation path. | Yes |
| B-270 | 2026-09-19 | The model-provider gate reads a recorded successful call, never a configured key. | The key the owner supplied authenticates against an account with no credit: the first request returned "Your credit balance is too low to access the Anthropic API". An environment-variable check would have opened the gate and un-parked four requirements onto work that cannot run, which is the queue advertising work nobody can start -- the failure the executor exists to prevent, arriving through its own gate definition. A probe cadence records real calls, so the gate opens by itself when credits arrive. | Yes |
| B-271 | 2026-09-19 | The monthly model ceiling is checked before the call, on a padded estimate that assumes the whole output allowance is written. | A ceiling discovered on the invoice is not a ceiling, and one that trusts an optimistic estimate is crossed before anybody notices. Budgeting for the usual case is how a ceiling becomes a target. An unpriced model is refused outright, because a call whose cost cannot be computed is unbounded. | Yes |
| B-272 | 2026-09-19 | Configuration may lower the model ceiling and may never raise it. | A ceiling an environment variable can raise is a default. The owner's CA$25 is the maximum the code will honour whatever the variable says. | Yes |
| B-273 | 2026-09-19 | Model prices are recorded as an assumption on every row they produce. | The per-token figures are the provider's published list at an assumed exchange rate, not a measurement of this account's billing. A cost that reads as measured when it was calculated is the same class of error as an unlabelled trend figure. | Yes |
| B-274 | 2026-09-19 | A proof example must point at a row in this database or it is not proof. | The standing rule against manufactured reviews survives on good intentions right up until a quiet week. A tester example points at a physical test, a customer example at a sale; a testimonial somebody typed is refused at the door. | Yes |
| B-275 | 2026-09-19 | The support service level is the minimum of its canonical and escalated figures, never an average. | One number over both lets a fast automated majority bury the slow escalated minority, and the escalated minority is where the unhappy buyers are. An untimed case is counted as untimed rather than dropped, because dropping it measures the cases somebody remembered to time. | Yes |
| B-276 | 2026-09-19 | Support response times are recorded once and cannot be revised. | A response time that can be revised is one somebody will revise, and the revision always goes the same way. | Yes |
| B-277 | 2026-09-19 | Timings are stored in an existing JSON column rather than a new one. | The deployed database is created with create_all, which adds tables and not columns. A new column would exist in every test and in no production row: a measurement that works everywhere except where it matters. | Yes |
| B-278 | 2026-09-19 | A funnel stage that killed nothing is refused, not warned about. | A kill gate with no kills either had nothing to judge or was not applied, and both mean the stage did not happen. A warned-about funnel is a funnel that ran. | Yes |
| B-279 | 2026-09-19 | An external observation can never be promoted into internal causal evidence. | The step is a single sentence and it is never noticed: somebody reads that textured stitches are trending, it goes in a brief as context, the next brief cites the first, and six weeks later it is something the company knows with nobody able to say who measured it. as_evidence() exists precisely so it can refuse by name. | Yes |
| B-280 | 2026-09-19 | A storefront takeover declares its revert date when it is planned. | The failure is not the Christmas banner going up late; it is the Christmas banner still being up in February, which says nobody is home more loudly than an empty shop. The end comes from the same calendar as the start, so it exists before anybody is busy. | Yes |
| B-281 | 2026-09-19 | Six brand elements may never change in a seasonal takeover. | A shop that resets its wordmark, palette anchor, photographic language and voice every quarter is not seasonal, it is unrecognisable, and the timeliness it buys is worth less than the recognition it spends. Policy text is on the list because those are promises rather than decoration. | Yes |
| B-282 | 2026-09-19 | The lead-time engine may retire a product class and may never stand down an occasion. | The owner's correction. "A 150-hour flagship cannot be finished for this Christmas" and "Christmas is closed" are different sentences, and a careless reader turns the first into the second. A retirement is recorded as one class, one occasion, one date, and two refusals stop a priority programme being stood down while any lane is open. | Yes |
| B-283 | 2026-09-19 | Capacity across make lanes is runway viability weighted by how soon each lane closes. | This makes the compression automatic rather than remembered. A comfortable lane with months of runway ranks below a tight one shutting in a fortnight, because the tight one is the opportunity about to be lost; and as a lane retires, normalisation hands its share to the lanes behind it. A decision that has to be remembered is a decision that gets missed in November. | Yes |
| B-284 | 2026-09-19 | A closed launch lane is not a closed occasion. | Past the point where a new listing can be launched and indexed, the catalogue already listed goes on selling until the buyer's own last make date. The programme moves to merchandising and holds its capacity floor, because releasing it there releases it in the fortnight the occasion earns the money. | Yes |
| B-285 | 2026-09-19 | A key is not a capability: the model gate reads a recorded successful call. | Verified twice over. The key authenticated against an account with no credit, and an environment-variable check would have un-parked four requirements onto work that could not run. When credit arrived the probe succeeded and the gate opened with nobody telling it to, which is the property the executor exists to provide, observed rather than asserted. | Yes |
| B-286 | 2026-09-19 | A self-change is graded by what it touches, never by what it is called. | The description is the part somebody chooses, so a spend limit adjusted inside a change described as a scoring tweak is refused as a spend change. An unknown surface grades upward to code, because defaulting downward makes "touch something unlisted" the fast path and that is the first thing anybody would find. | Yes |
| B-287 | 2026-09-19 | Each risk tier carries a cooldown and a rolling weekly ceiling, not only an evidence bar. | Six changes to one surface in an afternoon cannot be attributed to any of them, and the attribution is what makes the next change an improvement rather than a guess. Continuous learning with no ceiling is a company rewriting itself faster than it can observe the result, which is what #178 forbids by name. | Yes |
| B-288 | 2026-09-19 | The gate tier never promotes without a person. | Truth gates, policy, release logic, spend and publication are the surfaces where being wrong is not recoverable by reverting quickly. Everything else in the improvement department is allowed to be autonomous; these are not. | Yes |
| B-289 | 2026-09-19 | A cell's self-review is deterministic and proposes only from patterns in its own rows. | A model can write a plausible improvement hypothesis about anything, and a cell that proposes fluently every week looks exactly like a cell that is learning. Each proposal names the pattern and carries the rows behind it, and a clean record proposes nothing -- which is the correct output of a review rather than a gap to fill. | Yes |
| B-290 | 2026-09-19 | A capability profile is assembled from the rows, never stored beside them. | A second copy of the truth drifts from the first, and the drift is invisible because both look authoritative. Assembling costs a query and removes the failure entirely. | Yes |
| B-291 | 2026-09-19 | The words a buyer types are not the words a premise may use, and the two lists are kept apart. | "Easy" and "gift" describe nothing in a creative premise and are most of what a shopper searches for. Sharing one vocabulary between the creative gate and the search mapper for tidiness would break one of them, and it would break the one nobody is looking at. | Yes |
| B-292 | 2026-09-19 | A search facet carrying our own vocabulary is refused rather than flagged. | A facet filled with our language is worse than an empty one: it produces phrases that look like research. The aesthetic facet is where it leaks in, because it is the one we care about most. | Yes |
| B-293 | 2026-09-19 | A suite that exits clean while reporting no passes counts as a failure. | Nine suites written in one session contributed zero to the headline total because they printed a lowercase marker. Exit codes still caught their failures, so nothing was broken and the number was quietly wrong -- which is the harder fault to notice, and the reason the check is on the line somebody actually reads. | Yes |
| B-294 | 2026-09-19 | A queue assertion states a property, never a count of today's backlog. | "More than a hundred ready" passed for the wrong reason while the build was young and failed for a good one -- work getting done. What holds at every size of backlog is that parking costs the queue exactly the requirements that are parked and not one more. | Yes |
| B-295 | 2026-09-19 | A collection member is refused by the same distance function that refuses a duplicate concept. | One definition of "the same idea", used in both places. A second definition written for collections would drift from the first, and the drift would be invisible because both look authoritative. The closest pair is reported rather than the mean, because one derivative pair makes a derivative collection and an average hides it. | Yes |
| B-296 | 2026-09-19 | A seasonal team is a share of capacity or it is a name. | The failure is not a bad team; it is six teams each holding a slice of the same finite capacity, every one under-resourced by exactly as much as the others are notional. A quarter is reserved for evergreen work, because a shop excellent in December and absent in February is not a business. | Yes |
| B-297 | 2026-09-19 | The fast lane imports the release chain's stage list rather than restating it. | Every fast lane begins as a queue-jump and ends as an exemption, one defensible deadline at a time. A copy of the gate list is how that happens without anybody deciding it: somebody edits one, both still look right. A test also asserts the constant matches the stages the chain actually appends. | Yes |
| B-298 | 2026-09-19 | A re-merchandising move never increments the catalogue. | A recolour presented as a launch inflates catalogue size, release rate and the apparent breadth of a collection at once, and every number the company steers by moves with it. Anything touching construction, rows, gauge or the release hash is a new product and goes through the pipeline that has a physical test in it. | Yes |
| B-299 | 2026-09-19 | An unmeasured scoring dimension leaves the arithmetic and is named. | Filling it with a neutral value is the standard way a score becomes fiction: the number keeps its shape, loses its meaning, and nobody can tell by looking. Each score carries the share of its own weight that was measured. | Yes |
| B-300 | 2026-09-19 | Markets scored at materially different confidences are refused a ranking, not annotated. | Ranking a market scored on two dimensions against one scored on eight is arithmetic on different things. A caveat does not fix it: the caveat is read once and the ranking is read every week. | Yes |
| B-301 | 2026-09-19 | The operator credential is provisioned Railway-side so the export endpoint stops being closed to everybody. | `opsauth` refuses everyone when the token is unset, which is the correct default and also meant the one endpoint that can hand the owner their own database had never been reachable. The token lives in Railway and nowhere else: not in this repository, not in BUILD_STATE, not in an audit row. Its existence is recorded here; its value is not. | Yes |
| B-302 | 2026-09-19 | A dead letter whose defect has been fixed is re-driven rather than waited out. | The queue-health signal staying red after the fix is how a signal becomes something people scroll past, and the next red one costs more than this one did. Publication jobs are never re-driven from that endpoint, whatever is asked for: their dead letters are refusals working correctly. | Yes |
| B-303 | 2026-09-19 | The Etsy shop's existence unparked nothing, and the four requirements waiting on it were re-gated rather than released. | Marketplace Insights and seller-side offers are blocked by automated access to the shop, not by the shop existing. Unparking them on the good news would have put work in the ready queue that nothing can start -- the failure the executor exists to prevent, arriving as a reward. The gate stays, satisfied and empty, because a gate that has opened is evidence. | Yes |
| B-304 | 2026-09-19 | A seasonal transformation is routed before it is costed, and a palette change is a photograph. | Four seasonal palettes of a proven garment look like four products and are one. Treating them as products spends weeks on something the concept engine scores at zero distance from its parent, while the transformation worth engineering never gets made because the quota was filled by recolours. | Yes |
| B-305 | 2026-09-19 | An engineered seasonal variant with no emotional promise is refused. | It changes the object, takes a compile and a physical test, and names no moment a buyer would act on -- which is the creative jury's dominant failure on the existing catalogue arriving through the seasonal door. A technically perfect but boring product is a failed product, and a seasonal palette does not fix one. | Yes |
| B-306 | 2026-09-19 | A divergence against our own catalogue may never describe itself as a market gap. | The thing nobody has made and the thing we happen not to have made are different discoveries, and only one of them is an opportunity. With no observed listing the weaker claim is the true one, and borrowing the authority of a scan that did not happen is how an assumption becomes a plan. | Yes |
| B-307 | 2026-09-19 | Both the incremental and breakthrough lanes are held open by a floor rather than by intention. | Incremental work has a visible customer and breakthrough work has an argument, so the argument loses every planning round until the catalogue is a commodity. A floor is the only version of "maintain both" that survives a quarter. | Yes |
| B-308 | 2026-09-19 | Listing frames are judged on their rendered pixels, with the background inferred rather than assumed. | Build 1 shipped a truthful, invisible hero while the plan was correct, the renderer ran and the file was written. A check that hardcodes the brand's cream passes a blank frame the day the brand changes -- and inferring from the corners inverted on a full-bleed image, reporting 97% of one colour as 96% empty, which is why it is measured over the whole frame. | Yes |
| B-309 | 2026-09-19 | An image legible at listing scale and uniform at thumbnail scale is a defect, not a trade-off. | The search grid is where an image is judged. Detail that survives at two thousand pixels and vanishes at a hundred and seventy produces a listing nobody will ever click, and nothing else in the chain looks at that size. | Yes |

| B-310 | 2026-09-19 | The Etsy gate opens from a recorded successful read, never from two variables being set. | Etsy v3 refuses the keystring alone with "Shared secret is required in x-api-key header", so a half-configured credential returns 403 and is indistinguishable from a working one to an environment check. The probe runs ahead of the scan that depends on it, so the gate also closes by itself if the credential is revoked rather than being discovered as an empty catalogue. | Yes |
| B-311 | 2026-09-19 | The probe reads the application ping rather than the benchmark shop. | It demonstrates capability without reading data the mission has not decided to read yet, and it returns an application id rather than anything about anybody's catalogue. Capability and observation are separate acts and are kept separate. | Yes |
| B-312 | 2026-09-19 | A benchmark listing is routed by matching whole words, never substrings. | `"vest" in "harvest"` filed a throw blanket and an ear warmer as garments, and `"slippers"` missed "Slipper Boot". A missed listing shows up in the unclassified count; a wrongly-routed one is invisible forever, so the looser rule is the more expensive one. | Yes |
| B-313 | 2026-09-19 | The pod vocabulary may only grow from words observed in real titles. | A keyword nothing matches widens the table without widening coverage, and does it invisibly. The gap report exists to supply that evidence. | Yes |
| B-314 | 2026-09-19 | A form word always beats a motif word; a motif decides only when nothing states a form. | Adding "pumpkin" to the soft-sculpture pod sent a cardigan and a cushion to the specialist in stuffing firmness. What a product depicts is not what it is, and a season says when it sells. | Yes |
| B-315 | 2026-09-19 | Where several terms match, the earliest in the title wins; length and table order only break ties. | An Etsy title names the product it is selling first. "Beach Bag Pattern and Hair Tie" is a bag; "Ear Warmer / Head wrap / Headband" is a headband. Table order was deciding both, so a headband reached the specialist in chest grading. | Yes |
| B-316 | 2026-09-19 | A multi-pattern ebook is routed to a bundle pod, not to the product its title mentions. | A six-pattern collection is a bundle first. Routing it into a product pod hides the mechanism — and bundling is the one MJs uses most visibly: 29 of 438 listings. | Yes |
| B-317 | 2026-09-19 | Every scan re-routes the whole stored catalogue through the current vocabulary. | The scanner routes on discovery and skips unchanged fingerprints, so a vocabulary improvement would otherwise reach only listings the benchmark shop later edits. A fix that cannot reach the data it was drawn from is not a fix. It reads stored titles, makes no Etsy call and is idempotent. | Yes |
| B-318 | 2026-09-19 | A coverage gap is reported by naming the rows, never by counting them. | `unclassified: 58` sat in the dashboard and was not actionable. The same report's own 120-character truncation then hid two defects, so the display is part of the evidence, not decoration. | Yes |
| B-319 | 2026-09-19 | The blinded card carries no free text, only fields both sides fill from the same enumeration. | Our concepts have evocative premises and palette stories; a benchmark listing has none. Our prose against their keywords lets a judge separate the sides without reading either, so the run would measure register. The cost is a thinner comparison, and it is the honest one. | Yes |
| B-320 | 2026-09-19 | A run with lopsided position bias is discarded, never corrected. | Position bias is the classic failure of pairwise model evaluation and it produces a stable, confident, meaningless win rate. A correction applied to a judge that was not really reading puts error bars around nothing. | Yes |
| B-321 | 2026-09-19 | A benchmark listing whose title states no form or feeling does not become an opponent. | A default that appears on one side is a tell; one that appears on both is a fabrication about one of them. The resulting selection bias toward richly-titled listings is reported rather than hidden — 220 of 438 are usable. | Yes |
| B-322 | 2026-09-19 | The blinded run is a monthly cadence with no endpoint that triggers it. | It is the only cadence that spends real money per run. Twelve pairs is 6% of the CA$25 ceiling; weekly would be 27% for a number that cannot move that fast, and an expensive measurement is one somebody switches off. A request that spends the model budget should not be one URL away. | Yes |
| B-323 | 2026-09-19 | A blinded pairing is same-form as well as same-pod. | The first live run scored 11-1 for this catalogue by comparing rectangle throws to coasters and pillows, with position bias and sample floor both green. A blanket beats a coaster on perceived value whoever designed it. The confound cannot be corrected after the fact, only removed by construction. | Yes |
| B-324 | 2026-09-19 | A run that cannot reach the sample floor is refused before it spends. | Below the floor the verdict is `unmeasured` whatever the judge says, so buying it is paying real money for a result already known. The ceiling is a maximum, not a target. | Yes |
| B-325 | 2026-09-19 | A superseded measurement is marked, never deleted. | The 11-1 verdict is still served, labelled superseded and invalid with its reason. The record of a measurement that was confidently wrong is what stops the same mistake being made confidently again. | Yes |
| B-326 | 2026-09-19 | The ledger kind the model ceiling counts is one constant, not a literal. | `ModelGateway` wrote `kind="model"` and every ceiling reads `kind="llm"`, so a real call would have been invisible to the budget and the ceiling would have refused nothing. It never bit because nothing had ever constructed a ModelGateway. | Yes |
| B-327 | 2026-09-19 | A deploy re-drives its non-refusal dead letters once, and only once. | The fix for a defect is a deploy, so a deploy is the event that should retry it. The bound is the design: a commit that did not fix it kills the job again and it waits for the next. Without this a monthly cadence that dies on a typo waits thirty days after the repair. | Yes |
| B-328 | 2026-09-20 | Discovery starts from a proven-and-unserved arena, and the arena picks the form. | A generator left to its own preferences returns to the shape its compiler is fluent in. Forms are counted from observed listings in the pod, so the vocabulary comes from the market rather than from what compiles easily. | Yes |
| B-329 | 2026-09-20 | A form the engine cannot build is reported as an engineering requirement, never dropped. | Dropping it is precisely how a catalogue converges on flat panels while believing it is being selective. | Yes |
| B-330 | 2026-09-20 | A form missing from the buildability map is unbuildable, not unconstrained. | Six of twenty-two were absent, `stocking` among them, so the rules silently refused to build a Christmas stocking in a system that names Christmas a top priority. | Yes |
| B-331 | 2026-09-20 | A form carries a lane floor; a closing occasion changes the product mix rather than cancelling it. | Deriving the lane from physical size filed a beanie as SHORT and dropped Halloween/hats at 42 days. A lace shawl is light and slow; a bulky stocking is bigger and fast. | Yes |
| B-332 | 2026-09-20 | A cadence whose last run reported nothing to do is re-driven once per deploy, exactly like a dead letter. | A false negative wearing a success consumes its window and is invisible at queue depth zero. A cadence that has since run properly is never re-enqueued, so a run that costs money costs it once. | Yes |
| B-333 | 2026-09-20 | An empty result that contradicts observed evidence raises rather than returning empty. | `arenas()` returning no gaps against 438 observed listings is a defect signature, not a state, and the two are indistinguishable from a caller's side. A job that fails is re-driven; a job that succeeds with a false negative is not. | Yes |
| B-334 | 2026-09-20 | The armhole division is expressed as held stitches with a declared resumer, checked rather than trusted. | "Place 24 sts on hold" is a promise, and an unchecked promise ships live stitches nobody comes back for. Every hold is resumed exactly once by a piece starting on exactly the held count. | Yes |
| B-335 | 2026-09-20 | The writer states a division where it happens. | A divided pattern that reads "Rnd 3: sc 32" over a round of 48 is arithmetically correct, agrees with the twin, and is unfollowable -- the bag-of-pieces failure arriving through shaping instead of assembly. | Yes |
| B-336 | 2026-09-20 | Discovery weights its arenas by the priority programme's existing reservation. | Discovery is engineering capacity, so it obeys the 0.45 Christmas share the compression engine already holds rather than a second number invented for it. Round-robin would give a named priority one turn in twelve. | Yes |
| B-337 | 2026-09-20 | A prospecting pass says it is not a tournament. | The funnel's ideation floor exists because a small field is not a selection. Generating enough per arena to clear it would be optimising for volume, which the owner explicitly ruled out. | Yes |

| B-410 | 2026-09-20 | The interview instrument produces hypotheses and is structurally unable to produce findings. | The spec's own "convert responses into hypotheses" is implemented as a type, not advice: `status` is always `hypothesis`, and `promote()` requires evidence from an instrument that asked nobody a question. `NOT_A_TEST` refuses the three tempting alternatives by name -- another interview reproduces the selection rather than testing the claim, a larger survey measures the same bias more precisely, and a model agreeing is not evidence about buyers. A voluntary sample answers "what do the people who answer surveys think", and that population differs from buyers most sharply on the axis being measured: the delighted and the furious reply, the mildly disappointed majority says nothing. `sample()` reports that in fields rather than prose, because a caveat is read once and a field is read every time. | Yes |
| B-411 | 2026-09-20 | A sample is biased before anybody volunteers, by who is invited -- so selection rules that can see the outcome are refused by name. | Three ways an interview decides its answer in advance. Paying for it is guarded by putting the incentive on the invitation, requiring it identical across a round, and giving `Response` no incentive field at all: one chosen after reading a response is contingent however kindly meant, because contingency is a question of ordering and ordering is what is checkable. Asking too late is guarded by a 14-day window on "what nearly stopped you" alone -- a decision is retrieved, a hesitation is reconstructed, sincerely -- and reconstructed answers need memory-independent evidence to promote. Choosing who to ask is the dangerous one: it happens before any volunteering and every version of it can be said aloud as "asking our best customers". `left_a_good_review`, `repeat_buyer`, `no_refund_requested`, `seemed_friendly_in_support` and `high_order_value` all correlate with satisfaction and are refused. Legitimacy sits at the same seam the fake-review rule closes: no invitation without an order or tester record, and no response without an invitation, because an unanchored response is indistinguishable from an invented one. `MAX_SAMPLE` is 20 -- a large voluntary sample is not more representative, only more confidently unrepresentative. | Yes |
| B-412 | 2026-09-20 | #260 and #261 ship together because only one of them can see the people who did not buy. | The interview asks survivors of the purchase decision why they bought; everyone who left is outside it by construction, and their friction is the friction that costs most.
